# 1. Category Purpose

Category 9 lets the future TraderLink AI language layer recognize historical
questions about repeat trading, post-outcome sequences, threshold/rule
comparisons, and trader-recorded adherence or mistakes. It keeps a factual
history separate from an interpretation of character, intention, discipline,
or causation. Its `profit_giveback` entry is routing/reference-only: Category
10 owns the exact candle calculation, while Category 9 may reference that
result descriptively without creating a second calculation.

The category is a planning vocabulary layer. A future request must first use
server-authorized account scope and the Journal's current accepted evidence.
It may report a direct observation, a declared deterministic derivation, a
clearly labelled proxy indicator, a trader label, or that the requested
concept is not measurable. It must never turn trades, time, size, P/L, or a
label into proof of revenge trading, loss chasing, poor discipline, a setup
failure, a cause of a result, a prediction, or financial advice.

This category does not own the underlying execution ledger, P/L formulas,
outcome classifications, fees, position-size arithmetic, intervals, or
repeat-attempt identity. It composes those facts only after their owners have
selected eligible evidence and only where a behaviour definition supplies the
additional rule, threshold, or trader-authored label needed for the question.

---

# 2. Category Boundaries

## Included

The exact controlling list owns language planning for these behaviour families:

- frequency and within-day ordinal observations, including fourth-or-later
  performance;
- descriptive performance conditioned on an immediately preceding declared
  outcome, including a loss, two losses, win, large win, or large loss;
- performance of repeat attempts, while Category 8 retains the attempt
  identity and ordinal;
- explicitly labelled proxy indicators for loss chasing, larger size after a
  loss, and shortened waits after a loss;
- routing of `profit_giveback` language to the Category 10-owned exact candle
  calculation, with no duplicate realised-path behaviour calculation;
- comparisons of observed activity with an explicitly saved daily loss limit,
  profit target, stop threshold, or time cutoff;
- rule adherence, mistake frequency/cost, setup discipline, and plan adherence
  only when the applicable fact and trader label/review are explicit; and
- the mandatory evidence-class distinction: directly observed,
  deterministically derived, proxy indicator, user-labelled, or not measurable.

All result candidates must be built before result, date, ticker, or projection
state filters. An eligible result filter can select output; it cannot remove an
earlier sequence member, decision/incomplete barrier, or rule-applicability
fact and then renumber or reclassify later activity. Money comparisons retain
one declared gross or fee-complete net basis, one currency partition, account
IANA timezone date attribution, unit/instrument compatibility, formula/rule
version, and explicit open/decision coverage.

## Excluded

The following concepts remain owned elsewhere:

- primary intent selection, action safety, query routing, and completion
  policy: Category 1 and later policy categories;
- realized gross/net P/L, win/loss meaning, large-result monetary facts, fee
  coverage, currency partition, and result populations: Categories 2, 3, and
  5;
- quantity, position-size baselines, and the arithmetic of an increase after
  a loss: Category 6;
- raw timestamps, durations, between-trade intervals, and time after an exact
  previous loss: Category 7;
- execution/allocation facts, candidate ordering, `trade_sequence`, and
  `repeat_attempts`: Category 8;
- candle high/low context, intratrade MFE/MAE, and the exact
  `profit_giveback` calculation: Category 10;
- rule creation/versioning, rule reviews, annotations, tags, day notes, setup
  definitions, plans, and their UI or Journal writes: Journal Rules and
  annotations contracts; and
- trader psychology, intent, skill, edge, causation, recommendations,
  prescribed cooldowns, signals, and predictions.

## Cross-Category References

Category 9 references but does not redefine:

- Category 2 for a selected realized gross or fee-complete net P/L basis;
- Category 3 for outcome classification and performance aggregation;
- Category 5 for fee completeness and charge coverage;
- Category 6 for comparable size units/baselines and descriptive size changes;
- Category 7 for exact raw-UTC intervals and its predecessor/barrier rules;
- Category 8 for the pre-filter, account/instrument/account-local-entry-date
  candidate order, lifecycle ordinal, and repeat-attempt identity;
- Category 10 for the exact candle `profit_giveback` definition, calculation,
  data coverage, interval limitations, and capability state;
- the Journal Analytics contracts for `ready_closed`, factually
  `legitimate_open`, `needs_decision`, incomplete, unsupported, and excluded
  coverage; and
- Journal Rules/annotations for immutable rule version, scope, effective time,
  applicability, target/threshold/cutoff, trader review, and label provenance.

`profit_giveback` is deliberately routing/reference-only in Category 9.
Category 10 owns its exact candle calculation. Category 9 may describe a
Category 10 result as a historical association, but it must not duplicate the
calculation, produce a separate realised-path value, double-count the result,
or infer motive or causation. Until the Category 10 contract is accepted and
available, the Category 9 route remains `Planned` with explicit unavailable
coverage.

# 3. Planning Analysis

This planning-only draft establishes the exact 21-name Section 5.9 inventory,
its evidence classifications, and its ownership barriers before any canonical
records, language registries, evaluation cases, or coverage report are
produced.

## 3.1 Required Planning Questions

1. **What exact problem does this category solve?**

   It prevents ordinary behaviour wording from silently becoming a claim about
   motive or discipline. It routes each request to a declared historical
   observation, derivation, proxy, trader label, or explicit not-measurable
   result.

2. **What canonical concepts belong here?**

   Exactly the 21 ordered Section 5.9 canonical names in Section 4. No synonym,
   derived threshold, or additional psychology term is added in this draft.

3. **What related concepts belong elsewhere?**

   Underlying P/L/outcomes/fees, sizing, waits, candidate order and repeat
   attempts remain with Categories 2 through 8. Category 10 owns exact candle
   `profit_giveback`. Rules, reviews, annotations, setups,
   and plans remain their Journal owners; this category only recognizes a
   verified comparison or label.

4. **What data is required?**

   A server-authorized compatible account; stable instrument and compatible
   unit/currency partitions; current accepted executions/allocations and
   lifecycle projections; raw UTC entry/exit instants; account IANA timezone;
   declared gross or fee-complete net outcome basis; current projection and
   coverage states; and formula version. Threshold/rule concepts additionally
   require a saved, versioned trader rule with effective time, scope,
   applicability, comparator, governed event grain where applicable, and
   value. Label concepts require the applicable
   trader-authored label/review and provenance. No missing fact may be filled
   from a default, a later rule, or a story inferred from trades.

5. **Which deterministic tools will answer these requests?**

   Existing deterministic Journal read contracts can supply accepted
   executions, allocations, round trips, P/L/outcome populations, coverage,
   and rule/annotation facts when available. A future behaviour query adapter
   would compose those contracts read-only. The AI Chat interpreter/router,
   the named behaviour queries, and any rule-compliance aggregation are
   Planned; this inventory does not create them.

6. **Which concepts are directly observed?**

   Underlying accepted execution times, quantities, allocations, realized P/L
   components, saved rule versions, recorded applicability, and trader-entered
   labels/reviews can be directly observed. None of the 21 names permits a
   direct observation of motive, discipline, or causation; the table marks the
   narrow cases that expose a trader label rather than a derived claim.

7. **Which concepts are deterministically derived?**

   `overtrading_frequency` is the number of complete-coverage account-local
   active days whose factual zero-to-nonzero lifecycle-start count is strictly
   greater than an explicit nonnegative threshold `T`, divided by all
   complete-coverage active days in the declared period; a zero denominator is
   unavailable. `fourth_or_later_performance` is exact average selected-basis
   realised P/L for qualifying original ordinal 4-or-later `ready_closed`
   lifecycles: sum qualifying P/L divided by qualifying count, with zero count
   unavailable. Post-outcome performance and
   `repeat_attempt_performance` can be derived only after the full authorized
   candidate order is fixed, each predecessor/barrier is retained, a declared
   realised basis is usable, and the relevant sequence predicate is met.
   Saved-rule comparisons can be deterministic only against the applicable,
   versioned rule and its exact comparator/event grain. They do not establish
   why the trader acted.

8. **Which concepts are proxy indicators?**

   `overtrading_frequency`, `loss_chasing_proxy`,
   `size_increase_after_losses`, and `shortened_wait_time_after_losses` are
   proxy indicators only under an explicit, accepted definition. Category 10
   owns the exact `profit_giveback` calculation; Category 9 only routes to and
   may describe that result. These concepts report a bounded historical pattern and never
   prove overtrading, chasing, intent, emotion, discipline, causation, skill,
   or a recommended action.

9. **Which concepts are user-labelled?**

   `rule_adherence`, `mistake_frequency`, `mistake_cost`, `setup_discipline`,
   and `plan_adherence` require explicit trader-authored applicable labels,
   reviews, or rule records unless a separately accepted rule has a precise,
   machine-verifiable predicate. A deterministic rule comparison is not a
   substitute for a general discipline/mistake/setup/plan label.

10. **Which concepts are not measurable?**

    A concept is not measurable when its required threshold, rule, plan,
    applicability, label, predecessor, compatible P/L basis, fee coverage,
    candidate sequence, Category 10 result, or required evidence is absent. In
    particular, “large,” “too much,” “chasing,” “discipline,” “mistake,”
    “setup,” “plan,” “target,” and “stop” have no safe universal default.

11. **Which terms are ambiguous?**

    `overtrading`, `large win/loss`, `loss chasing`, `normal
    size`, `short wait`, `daily loss limit`, `profit target`, `stop`,
    `cutoff`, `rule`, `mistake`, `setup`, and `plan` can name different
    thresholds, rule versions, scopes, bases, or labels. “Gave it back” routes
    to Category 10 but can still require clarification of selected trade/scope
    under that category's contract. “After” can mean the
    exact immediate predecessor or any earlier result; only the former is a
    safe default for sequence metrics until a later approved grammar says
    otherwise.

12. **What defaults are safe?**

    Use no motive/default threshold. For a qualifying performance sequence,
    retain the existing Category 8 pre-filter candidate order and select the
    exact immediate candidate(s) before testing whether they qualify; never
    search farther back past a barrier. A time-cutoff request has no default governed event grain or
    inclusive/exclusive comparator; both must come from the applicable saved
    rule. Default to `ready_closed` realised performance output but show
    `legitimate_open`, `needs_decision`, incomplete, unsupported, excluded,
    and fee-incomplete coverage separately. A money result requires an
    explicit selected gross basis or fee-complete net basis; do not silently
    choose net where fees are incomplete.

13. **What conditions require clarification?**

    Clarify a materially unresolved P/L basis, currency/account scope,
    large-win/loss selected-basis threshold and comparator,
    overtrading/chasing/proxy definition, applicable rule/version,
    target/stop/cutoff interpretation, governed cutoff event grain and
    comparator, rule applicability, Category 10 selected entity/scope, or
    trader label/review. Do not ask a
    lower-priority question where authorization, evidence coverage, or an
    unavailable rule/label already prevents the result.

14. **What combinations are invalid?**

    It is invalid to filter candidates before choosing a predecessor or
    assigning sequence/attempt positions; skip a `legitimate_open`,
    `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or nonclassifiable barrier; count an
    open lifecycle as realised performance; mix accounts,
    currencies, instruments/units, rule versions, or gross and net results;
    apply a rule outside its saved scope/effective time; borrow a later rule;
    duplicate Category 10 `profit_giveback` as a separate behaviour or
    realised-path calculation; infer a label; or turn any
    historical association into causation, prediction, advice, or a required
    trading action.

15. **What evaluation coverage proves completion?**

    Remaining Section 5 records and later Sections 6 through 8 must cover every canonical name, evidence-class
    response, source-plan wording, ambiguous threshold/rule/label cases,
    gross-versus-fee-complete-net selection, account/currency/unit/timezone
    boundaries, candidate-before-filter and barrier cases, closed/open/decision
    coverage, the exact overtrading denominator and strict comparator, ordinal
    average arithmetic, first-breach daily rule semantics, later-lifecycle-start
    activity grain, cutoff event-grain/comparator requirements,
    no-rule/no-label/not-measurable results, Category 6/7/8 ownership and
    Category 10 routing without duplicate calculation, privacy-safe evidence,
    and negative cases for
    motive, causation, prediction, and advice.

## 3.2 Dependencies

- **Required earlier categories:** Category 1 for intent/coverage routing;
  Categories 2, 3, and 5 for selected P/L/outcome/fee facts; Category 6 for
  size; Category 7 for intervals; and Category 8 for lifecycle candidate order
  and repeat attempts.
- **Required database fields or services:** server-derived account scope;
  current accepted execution/allocation/round-trip versions; raw event times;
  account IANA timezone; stable instrument/unit/currency facts; outcome/P&L
  and fee coverage; ordered lifecycle-start and `ready_closed` closing-event
  facts; lifecycle/Data Decision coverage; effective saved rule version, scope,
  applicability, comparator, threshold, and governed event grain; and
  privacy-safe evidence references.
- **Required analytics tools:** read-only accepted-ledger and round-trip
  services, selected-basis outcome/P&L aggregation, candidate-order services,
  and later versioned rule/annotation applicability and behaviour-query
  contracts. Category 10 exclusively supplies the exact candle
  `profit_giveback` result.
- **Required UI context:** trusted selected account, date/range, ticker, or
  lifecycle context may narrow a result after candidate construction but can
  never broaden authorization or change sequence identity. No UI writes are
  authorized by a behaviour request.
- **Required user-defined vocabulary:** an explicit saved threshold for
  “large” plus its selected-basis comparator; a rule definition/value,
  comparator, event grain where relevant, and applicability for
  limit/target/stop/cutoff comparisons; and explicit user labels/reviews for
  mistakes, setups, plans, and general adherence/discipline.
- **Required external data:** Category 9 directly requires none. Category 10
  separately owns the versioned candle/provider facts and coverage needed for
  its exact `profit_giveback` calculation; Category 9 only receives that
  result through the accepted Category 10 contract.
- **Unsupported dependencies:** inferred psychology, intent, conviction,
  discipline, causation, predictive scoring, trade recommendations, prescribed
  cooldowns, missing account-equity/market data, and production AI Chat
  routing are outside this draft.

## 3.3 Risks

- **Ambiguity risk:** behaviour language often compresses an unstated rule,
  threshold, P/L basis, sequence predicate, or trader judgement.
- **Synonym-collision risk:** “gave it back” must route to the Category 10
  candle concept rather than create a realised-path duplicate; “repeat” can
  mean an attempt, an execution, or a ticker revisit.
- **Duplicate-concept risk:** Cat 8 owns repeat attempts/sequence, Cat 7 owns
  wait arithmetic, Cat 6 owns size arithmetic, Categories 2/3/5 own financial
  results, and Category 10 owns exact candle `profit_giveback`. Category 9 must
  not redefine or double-count any of them.
- **Unsupported-data risk:** a saved rule/label can be absent, inapplicable,
  superseded, incomplete, or unavailable; no universal large-result, target,
  stop, cutoff, normal-size, or normal-wait fallback exists.
- **Path/event-grain risk:** end-of-day P/L can hide an intraday first breach,
  repeated breach points can inflate a one-day violation count, and a later
  execution/close can be confused with a later zero-to-nonzero lifecycle start.
  Cutoff rules can also govern different event grains and comparators; none may
  be defaulted.
- **False-positive mapping risk:** post-loss activity or a larger next size is
  not proof of chasing; a later trade is not a violation without the applicable
  saved rule; a loss is not a mistake without a trader label.
- **Privacy and account-boundary risk:** sequence, rules, notes, and reviews
  may expose sensitive trading evidence. Results remain server-authorized and
  account-isolated, use privacy-safe evidence references only, and never expose
  raw broker, source, account, HMAC, or internal identifiers.
- **Causation/advice risk:** historical associations can be mistaken for why a
  trader acted or what they should do next. The category must state only the
  declared observation/label and its coverage.
- **Sample-size risk:** a few qualifying lifecycles, rule events, labels, or
  repeat attempts cannot establish a behavioural pattern. Return qualifying and
  excluded/barrier counts, not a confidence, diagnosis, or conclusion.

## Proposed Inventory Additions

None. Candidate ideas such as revenge trading, emotional control, confidence,
impulsivity, tilt, rule-break severity, recovery quality, and “good/bad
discipline” are not added because Section 5.9 does not list them and the
accepted evidence cannot infer them. A future proposal must define ownership,
evidence class, required trader fact, privacy boundary, and no-causation rule
before it can enter the controlling inventory.

## Proposed Removals or Merges

None. The 21 source-plan entries remain distinct and in controlling order.
Do not merge post-loss performance with its large-loss variant, rule-threshold
comparisons with general adherence, `repeat_attempt_performance` with Category
8 `repeat_attempts`, or Category 9's `profit_giveback` routing reference with
the Category 10-owned exact candle calculation.

# 5. Canonical Inventory Deliverable

All three batches contain the twenty-one controller-approved Version 1
canonical records `C9-BEH-001` through `C9-BEH-021` in exact
controlling-inventory order. All twenty-one remain `Planned`: deterministic
Journal primitives do not create the named Category 9 query contracts or an AI
Chat runtime. The records and their canonical names are approved and locked.

Every ordered concept below fixes its server-authorized scope before analytic
filters. Stable IDs may break raw-UTC ties internally but remain private.
Displayed evidence uses privacy-safe references and explicit coverage only.

## `overtrading_frequency`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-001 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Activity-frequency proxy |
| Canonical name | `overtrading_frequency` |
| Display name | Overtrading frequency |
| Exact definition | For one server-authorized account, declared period, and account IANA timezone, assign every factual zero-to-nonzero lifecycle start to its account-local entry day. An active day is a local day with at least one such factual start or a candidate that makes start coverage partial. For each complete-coverage active day, count factual starts from `ready_closed` and factually `legitimate_open` lifecycles. Given an explicit nonnegative threshold `T`, a breach day has count strictly greater than `T`. Return breach-day count divided by complete-coverage active-day count. Any `needs_decision` or incomplete candidate makes its affected day partial/unavailable and excludes it from both numerator and denominator with visible coverage; it is never silently skipped. A zero denominator is unavailable. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is an exact complete-day breach rate under an explicit threshold, labelled only as an overtrading proxy. It is not raw trade count, execution count, average trades per day, fourth-or-later performance, rule adherence, proof of overtrading, or evidence of motive, emotion, discipline, or causation. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed accepted lifecycle starts, projection states, raw UTC instants, authorized account, and account IANA timezone; deterministically derived local days, complete-day counts, strict-threshold breaches, numerator, denominator, and rate; proxy-based behavioural label |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Dimensionless rate from 0 through 1, optionally displayed as the mathematically equivalent percentage, plus breach-day count, complete-coverage active-day denominator, partial/unavailable day count, explicit threshold `T`, period, account timezone, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | A factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle contributes its factual zero-to-nonzero start to its entry day. It contributes no realised P/L. A `needs_decision` or incomplete candidate makes the affected day partial/unavailable; it is reported in coverage and cannot be dropped to manufacture a complete day. |
| Fee handling | Not applicable. This counts factual lifecycle starts, not P/L or outcome. Fees do not change start identity or the strict `> T` comparison. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

- Broader concept: Account-local activity-frequency analysis.
- Narrower concepts: Complete-coverage active-day denominator; strict
  lifecycle-start threshold breach; breach-day rate.
- Commonly confused concepts: number of executions, number of entries,
  `repeat_attempts`, trades per day, fourth-or-later performance, saved-rule
  adherence.
- Must not be merged with: `fourth_or_later_performance`,
  `loss_chasing_proxy`, `daily_loss_limit_violations`, or `rule_adherence`.

## `fourth_or_later_performance`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-002 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Within-day ordinal performance |
| Canonical name | `fourth_or_later_performance` |
| Display name | Fourth-or-later performance |
| Exact definition | First build Category 8's complete current lifecycle-candidate sequence within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and account-local entry-date partition. Order every candidate by first-entry raw UTC then stable round-trip ID and assign the original one-based ordinal before date, result, ticker, readiness, or other analytic filters. Retain state-labelled barriers and never renumber later candidates. Select ordinal `>= 4` candidates that are eligible `ready_closed` lifecycles on one declared gross or fee-complete net P/L basis and one currency. Return exact average selected-basis realised P/L: sum qualifying P/L divided by qualifying lifecycle count. A zero qualifying count is unavailable. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is average realised P/L for original fourth-or-later lifecycle ordinals, not the fourth execution, entry, fill, displayed/filtered row, fourth trade across instruments or days, fourth repeat attempt, or a claim that ordinal caused performance. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed lifecycle/account/instrument identity, first-entry raw UTC, account timezone, projection state, realised P/L components, fee coverage, and currency; deterministically derived partition, stable order, original ordinal, eligible numerator, denominator, and exact average |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Exact money per qualifying lifecycle in one currency, plus gross or fee-complete net basis, numerator sum, qualifying count, partition/date coverage, original-ordinal rule, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | A factually `legitimate_open` candidate retains its original ordinal and visible state but is not included in realised P/L. `needs_decision` and incomplete candidates remain visible barriers; exact pre-barrier output and partial/unavailable later coverage are reported without skipping or renumbering. |
| Fee handling | Gross uses accepted realised gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete Category 5 evidence for every included lifecycle in the same currency; fee-incomplete/conflicting/unsupported rows remain explicit coverage and are never substituted with gross or zero. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

## `performance_after_a_loss`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-003 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Post-outcome performance |
| Canonical name | `performance_after_a_loss` |
| Display name | Performance after a loss |
| Exact definition | Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and account-local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by first-entry raw UTC then stable round-trip ID before analytic filters. For each current candidate, select the exact immediate predecessor from that complete order before testing eligibility. The current candidate must be `ready_closed` for realised performance, and the exact predecessor must be `ready_closed` and classified as a loss on one declared gross or fee-complete net basis. Summarize the qualifying current candidates using an explicitly selected supported Category 2/3 realised performance measure on a compatible basis and currency; this concept supplies no unstated performance aggregation. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is descriptive performance for current lifecycles whose exact immediate predecessor is a selected-basis loss. It is not performance after any earlier loss, a losing day, two losses, a large loss, time/size after a loss, proof of loss chasing, or evidence that the loss caused the current result. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed accepted lifecycle identities, states, raw UTC instants, realised P/L components, fee coverage, account timezone, instrument, and currency; deterministically derived fixed partition, pre-filter order, exact predecessor, selected-basis loss predicate, qualifying population, and selected performance summary |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Units of the explicitly selected supported realised performance measure, with one compatible currency where monetary, selected gross or fee-complete net basis, qualifying sample count, barrier/coverage counts, partition rule, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | A current `legitimate_open` lifecycle remains visible but cannot contribute realised performance. If the exact predecessor is `legitimate_open`, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or nonclassifiable on the selected basis, it is an unskippable visible barrier. A current `needs_decision` or incomplete candidate remains coverage. |
| Fee handling | Gross loss/current performance use accepted gross facts. Net loss classification and net performance require fee-complete evidence under Category 5 in one currency. Fee-incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net evidence is nonclassifiable or unavailable coverage and cannot fall back to gross. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

- Broader concept: Exact-predecessor outcome-conditioned performance.
- Narrower concepts: Exact immediate predecessor; selected-basis predecessor
  loss; qualifying current realised-performance population.
- Commonly confused concepts: performance after any loss, performance after a
  losing day, `performance_after_two_losses`, `performance_after_a_large_loss`,
  `time_after_previous_loss`, `size_increase_after_losses`.
- Must not be merged with: `performance_after_two_losses`,
  `performance_after_a_large_loss`, `loss_chasing_proxy`, Category 7
  `time_after_previous_loss`, or Category 6 `size_after_losses`.

## `loss_chasing_proxy`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-009 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Post-loss descriptive proxy |
| Canonical name | `loss_chasing_proxy` |
| Display name | Loss-chasing proxy |
| Exact definition | Recognize a request for a descriptive, user-declared proxy specification that combines only explicitly named post-loss facts: Category 8 repeat-attempt/ordinal facts, Category 7 wait intervals, Category 6 comparable size facts, and Category 2/3 selected-basis outcome or realised-P/L facts. The specification must declare its included components, thresholds/comparators, Boolean composition, fixed authorized account/instrument/account-local-day scope, and gross or fee-complete net basis/currency where outcomes are used. Build the complete pre-filter lifecycle order by first-entry raw UTC then stable ID and preserve every open/decision/incomplete/flat/nonclassifiable barrier. Report the component facts and, only when the declared predicate is complete, its deterministic qualifying count or rate with denominator and coverage. With no complete declared specification, the proxy is not measurable. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is a bounded descriptive proxy assembled from declared historical facts. It is not a diagnosis of chasing, revenge, tilt, emotion, intent, discipline, compulsion, causation, future risk, or an instruction to stop or continue trading. A post-loss trade, short wait, larger size, or repeat attempt alone never proves loss chasing. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed accepted lifecycle, timing, size, outcome/P&L, rule-input, and coverage facts through their owning categories; deterministically derived pre-filter sequence, declared component predicates, qualifying count/rate, and denominator; proxy-based behavioural label; not measurable without a complete declared proxy specification |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Privacy-safe bundle of the declared components in their native units plus, only if explicitly defined, a non-negative qualifying count or dimensionless rate/percentage with exact denominator, selected basis/currency, thresholds, sample size, barrier coverage, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | A current factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle may supply factual start, attempt, wait, and accepted size components but no realised outcome. An exact predecessor that is `legitimate_open`, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or nonclassifiable for a required loss predicate is a visible unskippable barrier. Current decision/incomplete evidence remains partial/unavailable coverage. |
| Fee handling | Timing, attempt, and quantity components do not require fees. Any predecessor outcome, realised-P/L component, or money threshold must use one declared gross or fee-complete net basis and compatible currency. Incomplete net evidence cannot fall back to gross or be treated as zero. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

- Broader concept: User-declared historical behaviour proxy.
- Narrower concepts: Declared repeat component; declared post-loss wait
  component; declared size-change component; declared selected-basis outcome
  component; explicit proxy count/rate denominator.
- Commonly confused concepts: revenge trading, tilt, emotional trading,
  `repeat_attempt_performance`, `size_increase_after_losses`,
  `shortened_wait_time_after_losses`, losing streak.
- Must not be merged with: a mental-state diagnosis, `repeat_attempts`,
  `time_after_previous_loss`, `size_after_losses`, rule adherence, or trading
  advice.

The exact predecessor must be `ready_closed`, a selected-basis loss, and have an accepted final-exit instant. A predecessor that is `legitimate_open`, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or nonclassifiable is a visible unskippable barrier. Current decision/incomplete or missing-time evidence remains coverage. |
| Fee handling | Interval arithmetic is fee-independent. Predecessor loss classification uses accepted gross P/L or fee-complete net P/L under Category 5 in one currency. Incomplete net evidence cannot fall back to gross, and fees do not alter raw-UTC interval arithmetic. |
| Version | 1 |

## `daily_loss_limit_violations`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-013 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Saved-rule cumulative-path comparison |
| Canonical name | `daily_loss_limit_violations` |
| Display name | Daily loss-limit violations |
| Exact definition | For one server-authorized account and account-local day, require an applicable saved rule version with nonnegative daily loss limit `L`, effective scope, one selected gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L basis, and one currency. Order eligible `ready_closed` closing events by raw UTC then stable round-trip ID and form the cumulative selected-basis realised-P/L path. The first cumulative value `<= -L` is the breach; equality counts. A breached day contributes exactly one violation day regardless of later recovery or additional below-limit points. Do not use end-of-day-only P/L or count multiple breach points. Missing/inapplicable rule facts, zero eligible close-path coverage, or a path made incomplete by decision/incomplete evidence is unavailable or partial with explicit coverage. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is a deterministic first-breach comparison with the trader's effective saved daily-loss rule. It is not largest loss, end-of-day loss, number of below-limit closes, true account drawdown/equity loss, continued trading after a stop, general rule adherence, or proof of poor discipline. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed saved rule/version/scope, authorized account, raw UTC close instants, account timezone, realised P/L components, fee coverage, currency, lifecycle states, and Data Decision coverage; deterministically derived local-day partition, stable close order, cumulative path, inclusive first-breach predicate, and one-day violation count; not measurable without applicable complete rule/path evidence |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Boolean breach state and zero-or-one violation count per applicable account-local day; aggregate non-negative violation-day count only with explicit day denominator/period, plus limit `L`, basis/currency, first-breach time, rule/formula versions, and coverage |
| Open-trade support | A `legitimate_open` lifecycle has no realised close contribution and remains visible coverage; no unrealised P/L is invented. `needs_decision` or incomplete evidence that can affect the ordered daily close path makes the affected path segment/day partial or unavailable and is never skipped. |
| Fee handling | Gross path uses accepted realised gross P/L. Net path requires fee-complete Category 5 evidence for every included closing event in the same currency. Fee-incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net evidence makes the affected path partial/unavailable; it cannot fall back to gross or zero. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

- Broader concept: Effective saved-rule cumulative realised-P/L comparison.
- Narrower concepts: Inclusive `<= -L` first breach; one violation per local
  day; ordered cumulative close path.
- Commonly confused concepts: end-of-day loss, largest losing trade, daily
  drawdown, number of breach points, `continued_trading_after_stop_threshold`,
  general `rule_adherence`.
- Must not be merged with: `continued_trading_after_stop_threshold`, account
  drawdown, end-of-day P/L, or a discipline/mistake label.

Order eligible `ready_closed` closing events by raw UTC then stable round-trip ID and form the cumulative selected-basis realised-P/L path. It is not continued holding, later executions within an existing lifecycle, target violations by default, profit giveback, end-of-day P/L, general rule adherence, or evidence of greed, discipline, motive, or causation. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed saved rule/version/scope, authorized account, raw UTC close/start instants, account timezone, realised P/L components, fee coverage, currency, lifecycle states, and coverage; deterministically derived local-day partition, ordered cumulative path, inclusive first-target predicate, later-start order, and exact count; not measurable without applicable complete rule/path evidence |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Non-negative count of later factual zero-to-nonzero lifecycle starts per applicable account-local day, plus target `T`, basis/currency, first-attainment time, rule/formula versions, eligible-start count, and path/barrier coverage |
| Open-trade support | A factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle may contribute its factual later zero-to-nonzero start after target attainment but no realised P/L. `needs_decision` or incomplete evidence that can affect the cumulative path or later-start sequence makes the affected segment partial/unavailable and is never skipped. |
| Fee handling | Gross target path uses accepted realised gross P/L. Net target path requires fee-complete Category 5 evidence for every included closing event in the same currency. Fee-incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net evidence makes the affected path partial/unavailable and cannot fall back to gross; start counting itself is fee-independent. |
| Version | 1 |

## `time_cutoff_violations`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-016 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Saved-rule event-time comparison |
| Canonical name | `time_cutoff_violations` |
| Display name | Time-cutoff violations |
| Exact definition | For one server-authorized account, require an applicable saved cutoff-rule version with effective scope, account IANA timezone, explicit governed event grain, local cutoff value, and explicit inclusive/exclusive prohibited-side comparator. Convert each governed event's raw UTC instant through that account timezone and compare the resulting local instant with the applicable cutoff. Count each distinct applicable governed event once when it satisfies the declared violation comparator, such as `>` for strictly after or `>=` when equality violates. There is no default event grain or comparator. First lifecycle entry is governed only when the saved rule explicitly says so; execution, fill, allocation, lifecycle start, close, and submission are not interchangeable. A missing grain, comparator, timezone, raw instant, applicability fact, or complete event coverage makes the result unavailable or partial. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is an exact comparison with a versioned saved cutoff rule, not generic late trading, time-of-day performance, holding past a time, session classification, a default first-entry cutoff, general rule adherence, or proof of discipline, motive, or intent. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed saved rule/version/scope/applicability, governed event identity/grain, raw UTC instant, account timezone, cutoff, comparator, and coverage; deterministically derived local instant, violation predicate, distinct violation count, and applicable-event denominator; not measurable when required rule or event facts are absent |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Non-negative distinct violation-event count at the declared grain; optional dimensionless violation rate only as violation count divided by all applicable governed events, with zero denominator unavailable; rule version, cutoff, comparator, timezone, period, and coverage |
| Open-trade support | Governed factual events from `ready_closed` or factually `legitimate_open` lifecycles may qualify if the rule explicitly applies to their event grain. `needs_decision`, incomplete, missing-time, or unknown-applicability evidence remains visible partial/unavailable coverage and is never silently treated as compliant or violating. |
| Fee handling | Not applicable unless the saved cutoff rule itself includes a separate P/L predicate. Event-time comparison is fee-independent; any composed P/L condition must preserve its declared gross or fee-complete net basis and currency. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

  holding past close, after-hours trading, general `rule_adherence`.

## `rule_adherence`

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-017 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Versioned rule applicability and determination |
| Canonical name | `rule_adherence` |
| Display name | Rule adherence |
| Exact definition | For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit saved rule version and a stable decision/opportunity grain. For every candidate decision, preserve the rule's effective scope and factual applicability state before analytic filters. An applicable decision must have an explicit `followed` or `broken` determination supplied by the trader with provenance or produced by a separately accepted machine-verifiable predicate for that exact rule version. Adherence count is distinct applicable decisions determined `followed`; broken count is distinct applicable decisions determined `broken`; the denominator is all distinct applicable decisions. Adherence rate is followed count divided by that denominator. If any applicable decision lacks a factual determination, retain it in the denominator/coverage and return a partial or unavailable rate rather than silently excluding it. A zero denominator is unavailable. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is a versioned applicable-rule followed/broken measure, not performance, rule creation, a count of rule records, any threshold violation automatically treated as general adherence, or an inference from winning, losing, time, size, notes, or outcomes. It does not measure character, discipline, intent, or future behavior. |
| Evidence classification | Directly observed/user-labelled saved rule version, effective scope, applicability, trader determination and provenance, or directly observed facts consumed by an accepted machine-verifiable predicate; deterministically derived stable decision deduplication, followed/broken counts, applicable-decision denominator, rate, and coverage; user-labelled where the trader supplies the determination |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Followed count, broken count, all-applicable-decision denominator, and dimensionless adherence rate/percentage with unknown-determination coverage, rule version, decision grain, period, account scope, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | An applicable decision associated with a factual open lifecycle may be counted only when its rule applicability and followed/broken determination are already factual. Open, decision, incomplete, or unknown state never implies compliance or violation; missing determinations remain in denominator/coverage. |
| Fee handling | Not applicable to a non-financial rule. If the exact saved rule or accepted predicate uses P/L, it must declare one gross or fee-complete net basis and currency; incomplete net evidence makes the determination unknown rather than falling back to gross. |
| Version | 1 |

### Related Concepts

- Broader concept: Versioned rule-review measurement.
- Narrower concepts: Applicable-decision denominator; factual followed count;
  factual broken count; unknown-determination coverage.
- Commonly confused concepts: rule violations, number of saved rules,
  `daily_loss_limit_violations`, `time_cutoff_violations`, plan adherence,
  discipline.
- Must not be merged with: threshold-specific metrics, `plan_adherence`,
  `setup_discipline`, outcome performance, or a personality/discipline score.

| Inventory ID | C9-BEH-018 |
| Category | Behaviour Metrics |
| Subcategory | Trader-labelled mistake applicability |
| Canonical name | `mistake_frequency` |
| Display name | Mistake frequency |
| Exact definition | For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit versioned trader-authored mistake taxonomy/label and a declared stable decision, trade, or review grain. Every in-scope item must have an explicit applicability state. A mistake item is an applicable item with at least one factual trader-applied mistake label from that version; multiple mistake labels on the same item count once for the overall mistake-item numerator, while label-kind breakdowns remain separate. The denominator is all distinct applicable items at the declared grain. Mistake frequency is mistake-item count divided by that denominator; a zero denominator is unavailable. Applicable items with unknown label determination remain denominator/coverage and make the complete rate partial or unavailable rather than being silently excluded. A loss, rule break, poor result, proxy signal, or system guess is never automatically a mistake label. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is a frequency/count of explicit trader-labelled mistake items under a versioned taxonomy and applicability contract. It is not losing-trade frequency, rule-violation frequency, number of label instances, mistake severity, mistake cost, proof of carelessness, or a system diagnosis. |
| Evidence classification | User-labelled mistake taxonomy/version, applicability, item label, and provenance; directly observed stable authorized item identity and coverage; deterministically derived item deduplication, mistake-item count, applicable-item denominator, rate, label-kind breakdown, and unknown-label coverage |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Non-negative distinct mistake-item count, all-applicable-item denominator, dimensionless frequency/percentage, optional separate label-kind counts, unknown-determination coverage, taxonomy version, declared grain, period, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | An applicable factual open trade/review item may count only when the trader has explicitly labelled it under the declared version. Open, `needs_decision`, incomplete, or unknown outcome state does not create a mistake; missing label determination remains visible coverage. |
| Fee handling | Not applicable to mistake labelling or frequency. A label cannot be inferred from gross/net P/L or fee state. Any separate performance comparison must retain its own Category 2/5 basis, currency, and coverage. |
| Version | 1 |

| Subcategory | Trader-labelled mistake counterfactual comparison |
| Canonical name | `mistake_cost` |
| Display name | Mistake cost |
| Exact definition | Require an explicit versioned trader-authored mistake label/applicability fact linked to authorized stable evidence. A true cost additionally requires a separately approved, versioned counterfactual contract that supplies an authorized no-mistake comparison result for the same item, same declared gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L basis, and same currency. For each qualifying item, return signed cost difference as approved counterfactual selected-basis P/L minus factual selected-basis P/L; aggregate cost is the exact sum of compatible item differences with qualifying count and missing-counterfactual coverage. Do not invent, predict, infer, or model a counterfactual in this category. Without the approved counterfactual fact/method, `mistake_cost` is unavailable. The factual P/L sum for mistake-labelled items may be reported separately only as result on labelled mistakes and must never be renamed or interpreted as causal mistake cost. |
| Distinction from related concepts | This is an evidence-gated counterfactual difference for explicit trader-labelled mistakes. It is not factual P/L on mistake-labelled trades, total losses, mistake frequency, an AI estimate of what should have happened, lost opportunity, advice, or proof that the label caused the result. |
| Evidence classification | User-labelled versioned mistake/applicability fact; directly observed factual selected-basis P/L, fee coverage, currency, authorized item identity, and an approved counterfactual result/provenance when available; deterministically derived compatible signed item difference and aggregate sum; not measurable without the approved counterfactual contract |
| Capability status | Planned |
| Result units | Signed money difference per qualifying labelled item and exact aggregate sum in one currency, with gross or fee-complete net basis, qualifying denominator, missing-counterfactual/fee coverage, mistake-taxonomy version, counterfactual-method version, and formula version |
| Open-trade support | A factual open lifecycle lacks a final realised result and therefore cannot produce realised `mistake_cost`; it remains explicit coverage. `needs_decision`, incomplete, missing-label, missing-counterfactual, or incompatible-basis/currency items are unavailable and never assigned an estimated cost. |
| Fee handling | Factual and counterfactual values must use the identical declared gross basis or fee-complete net basis in one currency. Net requires complete compatible fee treatment on both sides. Missing/incompatible fees make cost unavailable; the calculation cannot fall back to gross, mix bases, or treat fees as zero. |
| Version | 1 |

# 6. Language Registry Deliverable

Registry Batches 1 through 4 contain complete Version 1 language coverage for
`C9-BEH-001` through `C9-BEH-021`. All 21 concepts remain `Planned`; these
registries do not claim an AI Chat runtime or named executable query. No
canonical name, formula, authorization scope, coverage rule, or evidence
boundary is changed. All 21 language registries are approved and locked.

## `overtrading_frequency` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account, explicit period, account IANA timezone, and
explicit nonnegative threshold `T`, divide complete-coverage account-local
active days whose factual zero-to-nonzero lifecycle-start count is strictly
greater than `T` by all complete-coverage active days. `ready_closed` and
factually `legitimate_open` starts count. A `needs_decision` or incomplete
candidate makes its day partial/unavailable and visibly outside numerator and
denominator. Zero denominator is unavailable. “Overtrading” is a proxy label,
not a factual diagnosis.

### Formal Wording

- "complete active-day lifecycle-start breach rate above threshold `T`"
- "ratio of account-local active days with start count strictly greater than `T`"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I exceed five new trades in a day during August?"
- "What percentage of complete trading days had more than three trade starts?"

### Trader Slang

- "Was I overtrading?" maps only to this declared threshold rate and must retain
  the proxy limitation.
- "How often did I trade too much?" needs a nonnegative daily-start threshold;
  it does not authorize a discipline or motive conclusion.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `OTF`, `OT`, or `freq` maps only in explicit daily lifecycle-start-rate
  context; a bare token requires clarification.

### Common Misspellings

- `overtrading frequncy`
- `over tradeing rate`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `overtraded >5?` still needs an explicit period.
- `AAPL over 4` may express a ticker plus comparison operator and must not be
  forced into overtrading language.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "an overtrading day" means one complete active day with start count
  strictly greater than `T`.
- Plural: "overtrading days" means the breach-day numerator, not all starts or
  all calendar days.

### Full Questions

- "For the selected period, what fraction of complete active days had more
  than `T` factual lifecycle starts?"
- "Show the breach-day count, denominator, percentage, and partial-day coverage."

### Commands

- "Calculate overtrading frequency with threshold four for the declared month."
- "Count factual lifecycle starts, not executions, and expose incomplete days."

### Sentence Fragments

- "overtrading rate above 5"
- "too many trade starts per active day"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use six instead" changes only threshold `T`; it does not invent or change
  the period.
- "Show the counts" requests numerator, denominator, and partial-day coverage,
  not execution rows.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant strictly more than five, not five or more."
- "Count new lifecycle starts, not fills or entries within the same trade."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare the selected two periods at threshold four" requires identical
  event grain, strict comparator, timezone rule, and complete-day policy.
- Report both denominators and partial-day coverage; do not compare percentages
  alone.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank months by overtrading frequency" remains Planned and requires explicit
  month partitions, the same threshold/formula, and deterministic tie policy.
- A period with zero complete active days is unavailable, never ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "Days that were not over threshold" means complete active days with start
  count `<= T`; partial days remain coverage, not non-breach days.
- "Not executions" preserves the factual lifecycle-start grain.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude partial days" may keep them outside the fraction only while reporting
  their exact excluded coverage, which the canonical formula already requires.
- A ticker exclusion cannot silently redefine this account-day metric.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Threshold five for the explicitly selected account period" is valid when
  authorization and the period are trusted; no missing date is inferred.
- Ticker, side, outcome, or readiness filters may not prune starts before day
  completeness is established.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Give me overtrading frequency, breach-day count, denominator, and incomplete
  days" maps to one calculation plus coverage inspection.
- A request for causes or what to do next is separate and cannot be answered by
  this proxy.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Too many trades" leaves threshold, event grain, and period unresolved.
- "Over four" may be a numeric operator; a validated ticker token or selected
  ticker context must not be reinterpreted as the overtrading concept.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker OVER" is a ticker-record request.
- "How many executions did I have today?" asks for execution/activity count.
- "Tell me why I overtrade and how to stop" asks for motive and advice that the
  rate cannot establish.

### Context Requirements

Require one server-authorized account, an explicit or trusted declared period,
account IANA timezone, and explicit nonnegative `T`. Do not invent today, this
week, recent history, a ticker scope, or a threshold. Authorization and stable
IDs are server facts, not user-supplied context.

### Required Data

- Every current lifecycle candidate's factual first-entry UTC, projection state,
  stable identity, authorized account, and account IANA timezone.
- Complete/partial active-day coverage including `needs_decision` and incomplete
  candidates; explicit period and nonnegative `T`.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe breach-day evidence references and numerator/denominator display.
- Equivalent percentage formatting and formula-version explanation.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit period boundaries under the account timezone.
- No ticker, side, outcome, result, or state filter may silently redefine the
  account-local active-day population in Version 1.

### Valid Groupings

- No named grouping is currently executable.
- Future non-overlapping period grouping must use identical `T`, event grain,
  timezone, coverage policy, and complete-day denominator.

### Valid Operators

- Strict daily comparison `start_count > T`, breach-day count, complete-active-
  day count, exact division, and optional percentage conversion.
- `>= T`, execution count, calendar-day denominator, and partial-day-as-zero are
  invalid substitutions.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_trend`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/negative `T`; invented period; mixed accounts/timezones; execution or
  fill grain; `>=` substituted for strict `>`; partial day hidden or counted.
- Motive, diagnosis, causation, prediction, discipline score, or trading advice.

### Default Interpretation

No threshold or period default exists. When both are explicit, interpret
"overtrading frequency" as the canonical strict complete-active-day breach
rate only. Return Planned/unavailable with coverage when required facts are
missing; never substitute average trades per day.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one highest-impact unresolved field at a time: threshold first, then
period. Do not ask for a ticker, cause, or preferred advice. A trusted selected
period may be reused; otherwise no date context is invented.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "What nonnegative daily lifecycle-start threshold should count as overtrading?"
2. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- No explicit nonnegative `T` or period; zero complete-active-day denominator;
  incompatible authorization/timezone; incomplete day coverage.
- Request to count executions/fills, hide partial days, infer motive/discipline,
  predict behavior, or recommend trading action.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic complete-active-day lifecycle-start breach-rate query
  over read-only current Journal lifecycle and coverage facts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Dimensionless exact rate from 0 through 1, optional equivalent percentage,
  breach-day numerator, complete-active-day denominator, partial-day count,
  explicit `T`, period/timezone, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Not applicable. Lifecycle-start identity and `> T` do not use P/L or fees.
- Fee state cannot exclude or add a factual start.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle contributes its factual start.
- A `needs_decision` or incomplete candidate makes its day partial/unavailable;
  it is not dropped to manufacture a complete non-breach day.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show both breach-day numerator and complete-active-day denominator; a small
  denominator supports no diagnosis, trend, cause, or prediction.
- Zero denominator is unavailable, not 0%; partial days remain explicit coverage.

## `fourth_or_later_performance` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID and assign original ordinal before analytic
filters. For ordinal `>= 4` eligible `ready_closed` lifecycles, return exact
average selected-basis realised P/L: qualifying sum divided by qualifying
count, in one gross or fee-complete net basis and currency. Open/decision/
incomplete candidates retain original ordinals and coverage; barriers are not
skipped or renumbered. Zero qualifying count is unavailable.

### Formal Wording

- "mean selected-basis realised P/L for original lifecycle ordinal four or greater"
- "pre-filter fourth-and-later lifecycle performance average"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did my fourth trade and later trades perform each day?"
- "What was my average P/L on trade four onward?"

### Trader Slang

- "How was I after the third trade?" maps to original lifecycle ordinal `>= 4`
  only when lifecycle grain and scope are clear.
- "Fourth trade fade" may describe an association but never establishes fatigue,
  overtrading, discipline, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `4+ P/L` or `4th+` maps only with explicit lifecycle-performance context;
  `4x`, `Q4`, or a bare `FOUR` token does not.

### Common Misspellings

- `forth or later performance`
- `4th trade perfomance`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL 4th+ net?` still needs an explicit/trusted period and fee-complete net
  evidence; AAPL is a current-result instrument context, not ordinal proof.
- `after 3 trades` is ambiguous between fourth lifecycle, fourth execution, and
  fourth displayed row.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "the fourth trade" selects original ordinal 4 in one fixed partition.
- Plural: "fourth and later trades" selects original ordinals `>= 4` without
  rebasing after filters.

### Full Questions

- "What was exact average gross P/L for ready-closed lifecycle ordinal four or
  later in the selected period?"
- "Show numerator, qualifying count, and barrier coverage for fourth-plus trades."

### Commands

- "Assign ordinals before filtering and average realised P/L for ordinal four onward."
- "Keep open and unresolved candidates in the original order."

### Sentence Fragments

- "fourth trade and later average"
- "4+ lifecycle net P/L"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes the P/L basis only with fee-complete same-currency evidence;
  it does not renumber candidates.
- "Only AAPL" selects instrument partitions/results without treating AAPL as a
  missing predecessor or resetting ordinals.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant the fourth lifecycle, not the fourth execution."
- "Keep original ordinals; do not make the filtered first row trade one."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare first-three versus fourth-plus average P/L" requires compatible
  selected basis/currency, original ordinals, denominator counts, and coverage.
- This concept owns only the fourth-plus side; a first-three comparator must be
  explicitly defined by the comparison query.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by fourth-plus performance" remains Planned and requires
  compatible per-instrument partitions, selected basis/currency, sample counts,
  coverage, and deterministic tie rules.
- Unavailable/zero-count groups are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "Fourth or later, not first three" selects ordinals `>= 4` after original
  sequence construction.
- "Exclude open trades" removes them from realised numerator only; their ordinal
  and coverage remain.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude AAPL results" may filter output partitions after ordinal assignment;
  it cannot renumber other candidates within any fixed partition.
- Excluding unresolved candidates cannot bridge a barrier or create exact later output.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net fourth-plus P/L for selected long AAPL trades in August" validates
  result filters after pre-filter ordinal assignment and requires fee-complete
  net evidence in one currency.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show fourth-plus average, sum, count, and unresolved coverage" maps to the
  exact average and its audit components.
- Asking whether late trades caused losses adds an unsupported causal request.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Fourth trade" may mean lifecycle, execution, fill, entry, displayed row, or
  fourth trade across all instruments rather than the fixed partition.
- "Performance" here is fixed to average selected-basis realised P/L; "net"
  still requires complete fees and currency.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show four trades for the validated ticker FOUR" is a ticker/record request.
- "How many fourth executions did I have?" asks for execution sequence/count.
- "Should I stop after three trades?" asks for trading advice.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account scope, stable instrument partitions, account
IANA timezone, an explicit or trusted selected period, and declared gross or
net basis/currency. Do not invent today, a recent window, a ticker, or net fee
coverage. Candidate ordering precedes all analytic filters.

### Required Data

- All candidate account/instrument identities, first-entry raw UTC, stable tie
  identity, account-local entry date, and projection/barrier state.
- Eligible `ready_closed` realised P/L on selected basis, fee completeness,
  currency, qualifying sum/count, and coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated current-result side/source filters and privacy-safe evidence references.
- Gross/net audit components and account-local date display.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted period and validated current-result filters may select output
  only after fixed partition/order/ordinal assignment.
- No readiness, result, date, ticker, or outcome filter may rebase ordinals or
  remove a barrier before numbering.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by validated instrument or non-overlapping period may operate
  on already assigned original ordinals with identical formula/basis/currency.
- Account mixing and filtered-row ordinal grouping are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- First-entry raw UTC plus stable-ID order; original ordinal `>= 4`; eligible
  selected-basis P/L sum; qualifying count; exact division.
- Filter-first order, fourth execution, count-all states in denominator, or
  zero-denominator-as-zero are invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`,
  `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Renumbering after filters; ordering by close/import/display time; mixing
  accounts/instruments/local days/currencies; open P/L invented; fees incomplete.
- Fatigue/overtrading diagnosis, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret "fourth or later" as original lifecycle ordinal `>= 4` in the fixed
Category 8 partition and "performance" as exact average selected-basis realised
P/L. No date or gross/net basis is invented. Zero qualifying count returns
Planned/unavailable, not zero performance.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one unresolved field at a time: gross versus net basis first, then the
exact period if no trusted context exists, then lifecycle-versus-execution grain
if wording explicitly conflicts. Do not ask for motive.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Should fourth-or-later performance use gross P/L or fee-complete net P/L?"
2. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"
3. If still needed: "Do you mean lifecycle ordinal, not execution number?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing period/basis/scope; zero qualifying denominator; incompatible currency;
  fee-incomplete net; absent order facts; decision/incomplete barrier coverage.
- Request to rebase ordinals, invent open P/L, infer cause/discipline, or advise
  a trade-count limit.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic original-ordinal `>= 4` average realised-P/L query over
  current read-only Journal lifecycle, outcome, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Exact money per qualifying lifecycle in one currency, numerator sum,
  qualifying denominator, gross or fee-complete net basis, original-ordinal
  rule, partition/coverage counts, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Gross uses accepted realised gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete evidence for
  every included lifecycle in the same currency.
- Fee-incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net remains coverage and never falls
  back to gross or zero.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A `legitimate_open` candidate retains original ordinal and visible state but
  contributes no realised P/L.
- `needs_decision` and incomplete candidates remain barriers; later results are
  partial/unavailable and never renumbered across them.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show numerator sum, qualifying count, open/decision/barrier coverage, selected
  basis, currency, and formula version.
- A small fourth-plus sample supports no fatigue, overtrading, causal, predictive,
  or advisory conclusion; zero denominator is unavailable.

## `performance_after_a_loss` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID before analytic filters. For each current
candidate, select the exact immediate predecessor from that complete order.
The current lifecycle must be `ready_closed` for realised performance; the
same exact predecessor must be `ready_closed` and a loss on one declared gross
or fee-complete net basis. Summarize qualifying current lifecycles with an
explicitly selected supported Category 2/3 realised performance measure in a
compatible currency. No earlier loss may replace a barrier, and no performance
measure, date, or context is invented.

### Formal Wording

- "realised performance conditional on the exact immediate selected-basis losing predecessor"
- "pre-filter predecessor-loss qualified current-lifecycle performance"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did I perform after losing the trade right before?"
- "Show my results when the immediately previous trade was a loss."

### Trader Slang

- "How was I after an L?" maps only to the exact immediate predecessor on a
  declared gross or net basis.
- "After losing" or "after a red trade" cannot infer revenge, tilt, emotion,
  discipline, or cause; display color does not establish a factual loss.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `PAL`, `after L`, or `post-L` maps only in explicit predecessor-performance
  context; bare `L`, `LOSS`, or `red` is ambiguous and may be ticker-like text.

### Common Misspellings

- `performence after a lose`
- `perfomance after previous los`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL after L net?` still needs a selected performance measure and explicit/
  trusted period; AAPL does not select or prove the predecessor.
- `after losing did better` leaves measure, basis, comparator, and period unresolved.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "performance after a loss" applies to one current/predecessor pair
  only when that exact predecessor qualifies.
- Plural: "performance after losses" summarizes multiple qualifying current
  lifecycles; it does not search backward to any earlier loss.

### Full Questions

- "What was my average realised P/L when the exact immediately previous
  lifecycle was a loss on the selected basis?"
- "Show win rate after exact predecessor losses with barrier and fee coverage."

### Commands

- "Select each exact predecessor before filtering and require that same trade
  to be a selected-basis loss."
- "Do not skip flat, open, unresolved, or fee-nonclassifiable predecessors."

### Sentence Fragments

- "performance after exact prior loss"
- "results after losing immediately before"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use average net P/L" selects measure and basis only if compatible fee-complete
  net evidence exists; it does not reselect predecessors or invent a period.
- "What about AAPL?" applies a validated current-result instrument filter after
  sequence construction; it does not remove predecessor barriers.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant the immediately previous lifecycle, not any earlier loss."
- "Use gross win rate, not net average P/L" changes the explicit measure/basis
  while preserving candidate order and coverage.

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare performance after losses with performance after wins" requires the
  same explicit supported measure, basis/currency, fixed partitions, period,
  sample counts, and barrier policy.
- Historical differences remain descriptive and non-causal.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by performance after a loss" remains Planned and requires the
  same selected measure/basis, valid instrument partitions, minimum visible
  sample counts, coverage, and deterministic tie rules.
- Unavailable or barrier-only groups are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "After losses, not wins" requires the exact predecessor to be a selected-basis
  loss; it does not exclude winning candidates before predecessor selection.
- "Do not include open current trades" keeps them in coverage and never changes
  predecessor identity.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude AAPL current results" applies only after complete order and exact
  predecessor selection; it cannot remove AAPL candidates from another pair's chain.
- Excluding open/flat/decision predecessors never permits searching farther back.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Average fee-complete net P/L after exact losses for selected long AAPL
  currents in August" validates filters on current results after predecessor
  selection and requires one compatible currency.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show average P/L, win rate, sample count, and barrier coverage after losses"
  may combine supported selected measures without exposing private IDs.
- "Explain why" is a separate request and cannot turn association into causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "After a loss" may mean exact immediate predecessor, any earlier loss, losing
  day, loss streak, same-ticker loss, or largest loss.
- "Performance" requires an explicit supported measure; "loss" requires gross
  or net basis. Neither a date nor a measure is inferred from page context unless trusted.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker LOSS" is a ticker-record request.
- "How long after the loss did I enter?" maps to Category 7
  `time_after_previous_loss`.
- "Did the loss make me revenge trade?" asks for unsupported motive/causation.

### Context Requirements

Require one server-authorized account, fixed stable-instrument/account-local-
entry-date partitions, an explicit or trusted selected period/current
population, explicit supported performance measure, declared gross or net
outcome basis, fee state, and compatible currency. Do not invent dates,
selected trades, ticker context, or earlier-loss search.

### Required Data

- First-entry raw UTC, stable tie identity, account/instrument/local-day
  partition, and state for every current candidate.
- Exact predecessor state and selected-basis realised P/L/outcome, current
  eligible performance facts, fee completeness, currency, and coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated current-result side/source/ticker filters and privacy-safe evidence references.
- Multiple compatible supported performance measures and formula explanations.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted current-result period, ticker, side, source, or other
  validated filters only after complete candidate order and predecessor selection.
- No state/date/ticker/outcome/result filter may prune predecessor candidates or
  search for the nearest qualifying loss.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by validated instrument or non-overlapping period may summarize
  already-qualified current lifecycles with identical measure/basis/formula.
- Mixed account/currency groups and group-specific predecessor searches are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- First-entry raw UTC plus stable-ID order; exact immediate predecessor;
  `ready_closed` and selected-basis loss predicate; explicit performance aggregation.
- Eligible/loss-first search, close-time order, gross-for-net fallback, and
  filter-first sequence construction are invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `summarize_performance`, `calculate_metric`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Any-earlier-loss search; skipped flat/open/decision/incomplete predecessor;
  mixed basis/currency; fee-incomplete net; invented measure/date/context.
- Revenge/tilt diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or trading advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret "after a loss" as the exact immediate first-entry-ordered predecessor
only. That same predecessor must be `ready_closed` and a loss on the declared
basis. No performance measure, gross/net basis, period, or ticker is defaulted;
return Planned/unavailable until required fields are resolved.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one unresolved field at a time: performance measure first, then gross
versus net basis, then exact period/current population. Do not ask whether to
skip barriers or ask for the user's motive.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which supported performance measure should I use after the loss?"
2. If still needed: "Should the exact predecessor loss use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period or current-trade population should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing measure/basis/period/scope; no predecessor; exact predecessor non-ready,
  flat, nonclassifiable, or not a selected-basis loss; current non-realised.
- Fee-incomplete net, incompatible currency, request to skip/search back, expose
  private IDs, infer cause/motive, predict, or advise.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-predecessor outcome-conditioned performance query
  over current read-only Journal lifecycle, P/L, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Units of the explicitly selected supported realised performance measure,
  selected gross or fee-complete net basis, compatible currency when monetary,
  qualifying sample count, barrier coverage, partition rule, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Gross predecessor/current measures use accepted gross facts. Net loss
  classification and net performance require fee-complete evidence in one currency.
- Incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net is nonclassifiable/unavailable and
  never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A current `legitimate_open` is visible but cannot contribute realised performance.
- An exact predecessor that is open, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or
  nonclassifiable is an unskippable barrier; current decision/incomplete remains coverage.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count, selected measure/basis, currency/fees, and each barrier class.
- Small samples support no loss-chasing, revenge, causal, predictive, discipline,
  or advisory conclusion; no qualifying sample is unavailable, not zero performance.
