## `performance_after_two_losses` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID before analytic filters. Select the exact two
immediately preceding candidates for each current lifecycle before eligibility
testing. The current lifecycle must be `ready_closed`; both exact adjacent
predecessors must be `ready_closed` losses on the same declared gross or
fee-complete net basis. Summarize qualifying currents with an explicitly
selected supported Category 2/3 realised performance measure in one compatible
currency. Never skip a member to manufacture two losses.

### Formal Wording

- "realised performance conditional on two exact adjacent selected-basis losing predecessors"
- "two-member pre-filter predecessor-loss-chain qualified performance"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did I perform after losing two trades in a row?"
- "Show my results when the two trades immediately before were both losses."

### Trader Slang

- "How was I after two Ls?" means the exact two adjacent predecessors only.
- "Down two then next trade" is descriptive sequence language, not proof of
  chasing, tilt, pressure, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `P2L`, `after 2L`, or `0-2` maps only in explicit lifecycle-sequence context;
  bare `2L`, `LOSS`, or ticker-like text is ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `performance after two loses`
- `perf after 2 lossess`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL after 2L net aug` needs a supported performance measure and validated
  period; the ticker cannot prune the predecessor chain before selection.
- `lost twice then?` lacks current population, measure, basis, and date context.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "performance after two losses" may describe one current lifecycle
  with one exact two-predecessor chain.
- Plural: "trades after two losses" means all qualifying current lifecycles,
  not all trades after any pair of historical losses.

### Full Questions

- "What was average gross P/L for current trades whose exact two prior
  lifecycles were both gross losses?"
- "Show win rate after two adjacent net losses with fee and barrier coverage."

### Commands

- "Select the exact two predecessors before filters and require both to be losses."
- "Do not skip a flat, open, unresolved, or fee-nonclassifiable middle trade."

### Sentence Fragments

- "performance after two straight losses"
- "results following exact 2L chain"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes all predecessor/current outcome-dependent facts only with
  fee-complete same-currency evidence; the chain stays fixed.
- "Only TSLA" filters current-result partitions after full chain construction;
  it does not search for two TSLA losses unless that fixed partition supplies them.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant the exact two trades immediately before, not any two earlier losses."
- "The flat trade between them is a barrier; do not call it two straight losses."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare performance after one loss versus two losses" requires identical
  selected measure, basis/currency, period, partition/barrier rules, and sample coverage.
- Association differences do not establish a compounding emotional effect.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by performance after two losses" remains Planned and requires
  consistent measure/basis, valid partitions, sample counts, barrier coverage,
  and deterministic tie rules.
- Missing/partial chains are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "After two losses, not one" requires both exact predecessors to qualify.
- "Exclude flats" cannot remove a flat middle predecessor; it makes that chain
  nonqualifying/coverage rather than allowing a backward search.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude open current results" affects realised output only; open candidates
  and predecessor positions remain visible in sequence coverage.
- A ticker exclusion applies after exact chain construction and never bridges a barrier.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Average net P/L after two exact losses for selected short AMD currents in
  Q1" validates filters after chain selection and needs complete fees/currency.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show average, win rate, sample count, and broken-chain coverage after two
  losses" combines explicit supported measures with coverage.
- Asking whether the losses caused the next outcome remains unsupported.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "After two losses" may mean exact adjacent predecessors, any two earlier
  losses, a two-loss day, a streak within filtered rows, or two losing executions.
- "Performance" and gross/net basis remain unresolved unless explicitly stated.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show two trades for the validated ticker LOSS" is a ticker/record request.
- "How long after two losses did I wait?" is an interval request, not performance.
- "Do two losses make me revenge trade?" asks for unsupported causation/motive.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, fixed instrument/account-local-day
partitions, explicit/trusted period/current population, supported performance
measure, one gross or fee-complete net basis, currency, and complete chain
coverage. Do not invent dates, tickers, current selections, or filtered streaks.

### Required Data

- All candidate first-entry raw UTC/stable tie identities, partition, state,
  and exact two-predecessor adjacency.
- Both predecessor selected-basis outcomes, current performance facts, fees,
  currency, eligibility, and chain/barrier coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated current-result filters and privacy-safe chain evidence references.
- Multiple compatible supported performance measures and audit components.

### Valid Filters

- Validated filters select current results only after complete order and exact
  two-predecessor chain assignment.
- No readiness/date/ticker/outcome/result filter may remove either predecessor
  or search for a cleaner two-loss pair.

### Valid Groupings

- Future instrument/period groupings may summarize already-qualified current
  chains under identical measure/basis/currency/formula.
- Cross-account/currency groups or grouping that rebuilds chains are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC plus stable-ID order; two exact adjacent predecessor selections;
  two `ready_closed` selected-basis loss predicates; explicit performance aggregation.
- Any-two-loss search, filtered streak, close-time order, or gross-for-net fallback is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `summarize_performance`, `calculate_metric`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Skipping any middle barrier; any-two-earlier-loss search; mixed predecessor
  basis/currency; fee-incomplete net; invented measure/date/context.
- Loss-chasing/revenge diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret "after two losses" as the exact two adjacent first-entry-ordered
predecessors only. Both must be `ready_closed` losses on the same declared
basis. No performance measure, basis, period, or ticker is defaulted; return
Planned/unavailable until required fields resolve.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one unresolved field at a time: performance measure, then gross versus
net basis, then exact period/current population. Do not offer an any-two-loss
search or barrier removal.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which supported performance measure should I use after the two losses?"
2. If still needed: "Should both exact predecessor losses use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period or current-trade population should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing measure/basis/period/scope; fewer than two predecessors; either exact
  predecessor non-ready, flat, nonclassifiable, or non-loss; current non-realised.
- Fee-incomplete net, incompatible currency, skipped middle candidate, private-ID
  disclosure, causal/motive claim, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-two-predecessor loss-chain performance query over
  current read-only Journal lifecycle, P/L, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Units of explicitly selected supported realised performance measure, selected
  gross or fee-complete net basis, compatible currency when monetary,
  qualifying sample count, broken-chain coverage, partition rule, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Both predecessor losses and any net current measure require identical
  fee-complete Category 5 evidence in one currency.
- An incomplete predecessor/current net fact breaks eligibility; gross never substitutes.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A current `legitimate_open` is visible but cannot contribute realised performance.
- If either exact predecessor is open, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or
  nonclassifiable, the chain is unavailable and neither member is skipped.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count plus every one-member/two-member barrier and fee class.
- A small exact two-loss sample supports no revenge, chasing, causal, predictive,
  discipline, or advisory conclusion; no qualifying chains is unavailable.

## `performance_after_a_win` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID before analytic filters. Select the exact
immediate predecessor for each current lifecycle from that complete order. The
current lifecycle must be `ready_closed` for realised performance; the same
exact predecessor must be `ready_closed` and a win on one declared gross or
fee-complete net basis. Summarize qualifying currents using an explicitly
selected supported Category 2/3 realised performance measure in a compatible
currency. Never skip an open, decision, incomplete, flat, or nonclassifiable
predecessor to find an earlier win.

### Formal Wording

- "realised performance conditional on the exact immediate selected-basis winning predecessor"
- "pre-filter predecessor-win qualified current-lifecycle performance"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did I perform after winning the trade right before?"
- "Show my results when the immediately previous trade was a win."

### Trader Slang

- "How was I after a W?" maps only to the exact predecessor on a declared basis.
- "After green" or "winner then next" describes sequence only; it cannot prove
  confidence, momentum, complacency, discipline, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `PAW`, `after W`, or `post-W` maps only in explicit predecessor-performance
  context; bare `W`, `WIN`, or `green` is ambiguous and may be ticker-like text.

### Common Misspellings

- `performence after a wn`
- `perfomance after previous winer`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `TSLA after W net?` still needs a supported performance measure and explicit/
  trusted period; TSLA does not establish predecessor identity.
- `winner then better?` leaves measure, basis, comparator, and period unresolved.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "performance after a win" applies to one exact predecessor/current pair.
- Plural: "performance after wins" summarizes multiple qualifying current
  lifecycles without searching backward for any earlier win.

### Full Questions

- "What was average gross P/L when the immediately previous lifecycle was a gross win?"
- "Show win rate after exact predecessor wins with fee and barrier coverage."

### Commands

- "Select the exact predecessor before filters and require that same trade to be a win."
- "Keep open, unresolved, flat, and fee-nonclassifiable predecessors as barriers."

### Sentence Fragments

- "performance after exact prior win"
- "results after winning immediately before"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use average net P/L" selects measure/basis only with fee-complete same-
  currency evidence; it never reselects a predecessor or period.
- "What about NVDA?" applies a validated current-result instrument filter after
  sequence construction and cannot erase a barrier.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant the immediately previous lifecycle, not any earlier win."
- "Use gross win rate, not net average P/L" changes measure/basis only.

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare performance after wins with performance after losses" requires the
  same supported measure, basis/currency, partitions, period, samples, and coverage.
- No observed difference establishes confidence, momentum, or causation.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by performance after a win" remains Planned and requires
  identical measure/basis, valid instrument partitions, sample counts, coverage,
  and deterministic tie rules.
- Missing/partial groups are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "After wins, not losses" requires the exact predecessor to be a selected-basis win.
- "Do not include open current trades" keeps them in visible coverage and does
  not alter predecessor identity.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude NVDA current results" applies after complete order and exact
  predecessor selection; it cannot remove candidates from another chain.
- Excluding flat/open/decision predecessors never permits searching farther back.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Average fee-complete net P/L after exact wins for selected long NVDA currents
  in May" applies filters after predecessor selection and requires one currency.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show average P/L, win rate, count, and barrier coverage after wins" combines
  explicit supported measures with coverage.
- "Explain why winners changed me" is a separate unsupported motive/causal request.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "After a win" may mean exact immediate predecessor, any earlier win, winning
  day, win streak, same-ticker win, or largest win.
- "Performance" requires a supported measure; "win" requires gross or net basis.
  No date or selected ticker is invented.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker WIN" is a ticker-record request.
- "How long after the win did I enter?" is a Category 7 interval request.
- "Did winning make me overconfident?" asks for unsupported motive/causation.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, fixed instrument/account-local-day
partitions, explicit/trusted period/current population, supported performance
measure, declared gross or net outcome basis, fees, and currency. Do not invent
dates, current trades, tickers, or an earlier-win search.

### Required Data

- Every candidate's first-entry raw UTC/stable tie identity, fixed partition,
  projection state, and exact immediate predecessor.
- Predecessor selected-basis win classification, current eligible performance
  facts, fee completeness, currency, and coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated current-result filters and privacy-safe evidence references.
- Multiple compatible supported performance measures and formula details.

### Valid Filters

- Validated current-result period/ticker/side/source filters only after complete
  order and exact predecessor selection.
- No readiness/date/ticker/outcome/result filter may prune candidates or search
  for the nearest earlier win.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by instrument or non-overlapping period may summarize already-
  qualified currents under identical measure/basis/formula.
- Mixed account/currency grouping and group-specific predecessor searches are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- First-entry raw UTC plus stable-ID order; exact immediate predecessor;
  `ready_closed` selected-basis win predicate; explicit performance aggregation.
- Eligible/win-first search, close-time order, filter-first sequence, and gross-
  for-net fallback are invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `summarize_performance`, `calculate_metric`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Any-earlier-win search; skipped flat/open/decision/incomplete predecessor;
  mixed basis/currency; fee-incomplete net; invented measure/date/context.
- Confidence/momentum diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret "after a win" as the exact immediate first-entry-ordered predecessor
only. That predecessor must be `ready_closed` and a win on the declared basis.
No performance measure, basis, period, or ticker is defaulted; return Planned/
unavailable until required fields resolve.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one unresolved field at a time: performance measure first, then gross
versus net basis, then exact period/current population. Do not ask whether to
skip a barrier or ask for motive.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which supported performance measure should I use after the win?"
2. If still needed: "Should the exact predecessor win use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period or current-trade population should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing measure/basis/period/scope; no predecessor; exact predecessor non-ready,
  flat, nonclassifiable, or not a selected-basis win; current non-realised.
- Fee-incomplete net, incompatible currency, request to skip/search back, expose
  private IDs, infer confidence/cause, predict, or advise.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-predecessor outcome-conditioned performance query
  over current read-only Journal lifecycle, P/L, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Units of the explicitly selected supported realised performance measure,
  selected gross or fee-complete net basis, compatible currency when monetary,
  qualifying sample count, barrier coverage, partition rule, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Gross predecessor/current measures use accepted gross facts. Net win
  classification and net performance require fee-complete evidence in one currency.
- Incomplete/conflicting/unsupported net is nonclassifiable/unavailable and
  never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A current `legitimate_open` is visible but cannot contribute realised performance.
- An exact predecessor that is open, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, or
  nonclassifiable is an unskippable barrier; current decision/incomplete remains coverage.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count, selected measure/basis, currency/fees, and each barrier class.
- Small samples support no confidence, momentum, causal, predictive, discipline,
  or advisory conclusion; no qualifying sample is unavailable, not zero performance.

## `performance_after_a_large_win` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order every current lifecycle candidate by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID before analytic filters. Select the exact
immediate predecessor for each current lifecycle before eligibility testing.
The current lifecycle must be `ready_closed`. The predecessor must be
`ready_closed`, a win, and satisfy an explicit user-selected large-win P/L
threshold and comparator on one declared gross or fee-complete net basis and
currency. Summarize qualifying currents with an explicitly selected supported
Category 2/3 realised performance measure compatible with that basis/currency.
No threshold, comparator, basis, date, or context is invented.

### Formal Wording

- "performance conditional on an exact immediate predecessor satisfying the selected-basis large-win predicate"
- "pre-filter predecessor-win threshold-qualified realised performance"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did I perform after a win over $500?"
- "Show my results when the trade right before was a large net winner."

### Trader Slang

- "How was I after a big W?" needs an explicit monetary threshold, comparator,
  P/L basis, and currency.
- "After a banger" or "after a home run" never establishes confidence,
  complacency, motive, causation, or a universal meaning of large.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `PALW`, `big W`, or `post-LW` maps only in explicit threshold-conditioned
  predecessor-performance context; bare `LW`, `WIN`, or ticker-like text is ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `performence after a larg win`
- `perf after big winer`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL after big W net?` still needs the currency threshold/comparator,
  performance measure, and period; AAPL cannot select or prove the predecessor.
- `after 500 winner` is ambiguous about currency, gross/net basis, and whether
  equality counts.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "performance after a large win" describes one exact predecessor/current pair.
- Plural: "performance after large wins" summarizes qualifying currents without
  searching backward for any earlier threshold winner.

### Full Questions

- "What was average realised P/L after an exact predecessor gross win greater
  than $500 USD?"
- "Show win rate after exact net wins at least the declared threshold with fee coverage."

### Commands

- "Select the exact predecessor before filtering, then apply the declared large-win predicate."
- "Do not skip flat, open, unresolved, or fee-nonclassifiable predecessors."

### Sentence Fragments

- "performance after big win over 500"
- "results after exact threshold winner"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Make it $1,000" changes only the money threshold in the already declared
  currency; it does not change basis, comparator, predecessor, or period.
- "Count equality" changes the comparator to the explicitly requested inclusive
  form and requires recalculation with unchanged scope.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant net wins, not gross wins."
- "Use strictly greater than $500, not at least $500."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare performance after large wins versus all wins" requires identical
  selected measure, basis/currency, period, partitions, and coverage; the
  large-win side additionally preserves its threshold/comparator.
- Any difference is descriptive and non-causal.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by performance after large wins" remains Planned and requires
  identical threshold/comparator/basis/currency, sample counts, coverage, and tie rules.
- Missing/zero-sample groups are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "After large wins, not ordinary wins" applies the exact threshold predicate
  after predecessor selection; it does not filter candidates first.
- "Exclude equality" means strict comparison only when explicitly requested.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude current AAPL results" filters current outputs after exact predecessor
  selection and cannot erase a barrier.
- Excluding fee-incomplete or open predecessors never permits searching farther back.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Average net P/L after exact USD wins of at least $1,000 for selected long
  NVDA currents in May" validates current filters after sequence construction
  and requires fee-complete net evidence.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show average, count, threshold, comparator, and barrier coverage after large
  wins" combines the selected measure with audit components.
- Asking whether a large win caused overconfidence remains unsupported.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Large win" leaves P/L basis, currency amount, comparator, and possibly
  signed versus percentage threshold unresolved.
- "Performance" requires a supported measure; no period or selected ticker is inferred.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker WIN" is a ticker/record request.
- "What was my largest win?" maps to Category 2 largest-win language.
- "Did a big win make me careless?" asks for unsupported motive/causation.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, fixed instrument/account-local-day
partitions, explicit/trusted period/current population, supported performance
measure, threshold, comparator, gross or net basis, currency, and fee coverage.
Do not invent a threshold, date, ticker, selected trade, or large-win meaning.

### Required Data

- Complete candidate first-entry raw UTC/stable tie identities, fixed partition,
  projection state, and exact immediate predecessor.
- Predecessor selected-basis P/L/outcome, explicit threshold/comparator/currency,
  current performance facts, fees, and barrier coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated current-result filters and privacy-safe evidence references.
- Threshold audit display and multiple compatible supported performance measures.

### Valid Filters

- Validated current-result period/ticker/side/source filters only after complete
  order and exact predecessor selection.
- No threshold/outcome/readiness/date/ticker filter may prune predecessor candidates.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by instrument or non-overlapping period may summarize already-
  qualified currents with identical threshold/comparator/basis/formula.
- Mixed currency/account or group-specific predecessor search is invalid.

### Valid Operators

- First-entry UTC plus stable-ID order; exact predecessor; `ready_closed` win;
  explicit signed P/L threshold comparator; explicit performance aggregation.
- Eligible-first search, implicit `>=`, cross-currency comparison, or gross-for-net fallback is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `summarize_performance`, `calculate_metric`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned for this named Category 9 concept.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing threshold/comparator/basis/currency; skipped barrier; mixed bases or
  currencies; fee-incomplete net; invented measure/date/context.
- Confidence/complacency diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

No universal large-win threshold, comparator, basis, currency, performance
measure, or period exists. Preserve the exact immediate predecessor contract
and return Planned/unavailable until every required field is explicit or trusted.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one unresolved field at a time: gross versus net basis, threshold
amount/currency, inclusive versus strict comparator, performance measure, then
period/current population. Never ask for motive.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Should the large win be measured by gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
2. If still needed: "What exact currency amount should define a large win?"
3. If still needed: "Should equality count, or only values strictly above it?"
4. If still needed: "Which supported performance measure should I summarize?"
5. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing threshold/comparator/basis/currency/measure/period; exact predecessor
  non-ready, flat, nonclassifiable, or below predicate; current non-realised.
- Fee-incomplete net, incompatible currency, skipped barrier, private-ID request,
  causal/motive claim, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-predecessor threshold-win performance query over
  current read-only Journal lifecycle, P/L, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Units of selected realised performance measure plus threshold money amount,
  comparator, one currency, gross or fee-complete net basis, sample/coverage
  counts, partition rule, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Threshold predicate and current performance preserve one declared basis.
- Net requires fee-complete evidence in one currency; incomplete net never falls
  back to gross or another currency.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A current `legitimate_open` is visible but cannot contribute realised performance.
- An exact predecessor open, `needs_decision`, incomplete, flat, nonclassifiable,
  or threshold-incompatible is an unskippable barrier.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count and threshold/basis/currency/barrier coverage.
- Small samples support no confidence, complacency, causal, predictive, or
  advisory conclusion; no qualifying sample is unavailable.

## `performance_after_a_large_loss` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and
account-local entry-date partition, order every current lifecycle candidate by
first-entry raw UTC then stable ID before analytic filters. Select the exact
immediate predecessor before eligibility testing. The current lifecycle must

- "pre-filter predecessor-loss threshold-qualified realised performance"

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Did the big loss make me revenge trade?" asks for unsupported motive/causation.

### Valid Groupings

- Future instrument/period grouping only with identical predicate, measure,
  basis, currency, formula, and coverage.
- Mixed currency/account or rebuilt group-specific chains are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC/stable-ID order; exact predecessor; selected-basis loss; explicit
  signed or magnitude representation; declared comparator; performance aggregation.
- Implicit absolute value, implicit equality, cross-currency, or gross-for-net fallback is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `summarize_performance`, `calculate_metric`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing representation/threshold/comparator/basis/currency; skipped barrier;
  mixed bases/currencies; fee-incomplete net; invented measure/date/context.
- Revenge/fear diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

No universal large-loss representation, threshold, comparator, basis, currency,
performance measure, or period exists. Preserve the exact predecessor and
return Planned/unavailable until all required fields resolve.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: signed P/L versus absolute magnitude, gross versus
net, threshold amount/currency, comparator, performance measure, then period.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Should large loss use signed P/L or absolute loss magnitude?"
2. If still needed: "Should it use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact currency amount should define large loss?"
4. If still needed: "Should equality count, or only values strictly beyond it?"
5. If still needed: "Which supported performance measure should I summarize?"
6. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing representation/threshold/comparator/basis/currency/measure/period;
  exact predecessor non-ready, flat, nonclassifiable, or predicate-failing.
- Fee-incomplete net, incompatible currency, skipped barrier, private-ID request,
  motive/causal claim, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-predecessor threshold-loss performance query over
  current read-only Journal lifecycle, P/L, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Selected performance units plus threshold amount/comparator/representation,
  one currency, gross or fee-complete net basis, samples, barrier coverage,
  partition rule, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Predicate and current performance preserve one basis and currency.
- Net requires complete fees; missing net never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Current `legitimate_open` remains visible but cannot provide realised performance.
- Open/decision/incomplete/flat/nonclassifiable or predicate-incompatible exact
  predecessor is an unskippable barrier.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show predicate, qualifying count, basis/currency/fees, and every barrier class.
- Small samples establish no chasing, revenge, cause, prediction, or advice;
  zero qualifying sample is unavailable.

## `repeat_attempt_performance` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Use Category 8's complete current lifecycle-candidate sequence within each
fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and account-local entry-
date partition. Order every candidate by first-entry raw UTC then stable ID and
assign attempt ordinal before analytic filters. Ordinal 1 is initial; ordinal
`>= 2` is repeat. For eligible `ready_closed` repeat attempts, return exact
average selected-basis realised P/L: qualifying sum divided by qualifying
repeat-attempt count in one gross or fee-complete net basis/currency. Zero
denominator is unavailable. `needs_decision` and incomplete candidates are
barriers; later ordinals/results are partial/unavailable and never renumbered.

### Formal Wording

- "mean selected-basis realised P/L for Category 8 ordinal-two-or-later attempts"
- "barrier-preserving repeat-attempt performance average"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How did my second try and later tries perform on the same ticker that day?"
- "What was average P/L on repeat attempts?"

### Trader Slang

- "How did my re-entries do?" maps only when it means Category 8 zero-to-nonzero
  repeat lifecycle attempts, not fills or scale-ins.
- "Second crack" and "another shot" describe ordinal only; they do not prove
  chasing, revenge, persistence, motive, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `RAP`, `2+ attempts`, or `retry P/L` maps only in explicit lifecycle-attempt
  context; bare `RETRY` or ticker-like tokens remain ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `repeat atempt performence`
- `reentry perfomance`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL retry net aug?` needs trusted period and basis; AAPL does not permit
  filtered renumbering.
- `second entry did better` may mean a fill/scale-in rather than repeat lifecycle.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "repeat-attempt performance" may describe one ordinal-2-or-later lifecycle.
- Plural: "repeat attempts" includes all qualifying original ordinals `>= 2`
  within each fixed partition.

### Full Questions

- "What was exact average net P/L for fee-complete ready-closed repeat attempts?"
- "Show repeat-attempt sum, denominator, ordinal rule, and barrier coverage."

### Commands

- "Use Category 8 attempt ordinals and average P/L for ordinal two onward."
- "Do not count executions, scale-ins, or renumber after filters."

### Sentence Fragments

- "repeat attempt average P/L"
- "second try and later performance"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes P/L basis only with fee-complete same-currency evidence.
- "Only AAPL" selects fixed instrument partitions/results but never rebuilds ordinals.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant the second lifecycle attempt, not the second fill."
- "Keep the unresolved attempt as a barrier; do not call the next one attempt two."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare first attempts with repeat attempts" requires identical basis/
  currency/formula, original Category 8 ordinals, samples, and barrier coverage.
- This concept owns only repeat-attempt average, not a causal repeat effect.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by repeat-attempt performance" remains Planned and requires
  fixed partitions, identical basis/formula, denominators, coverage, and tie rules.
- Zero-count/partial groups are unavailable, not zero-ranked.

### Negated Wording

- "Repeat attempts, not initial attempts" selects original ordinal `>= 2`.
- "Not scale-ins" preserves zero-to-nonzero lifecycle-attempt grain.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude open repeats" removes them from realised numerator only; ordinal and
  coverage remain.
- Excluding an unresolved candidate never permits renumbering across it.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net repeat-attempt P/L for selected AAPL days in August" applies filters
  after Category 8 partition/order/ordinal and requires complete fees/currency.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show average, sum, count, attempt ordinals, and barriers" combines exact
  metric audit fields with coverage.
- "Explain why retries lost" cannot infer cause or motive.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Re-entry" may mean repeat lifecycle, fill, add, scale-in, or entry after exit
  without same-day fixed partition.
- "Performance" is exact average selected-basis realised P/L here; basis/period
  remain unresolved unless explicit/trusted.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show executions for the validated ticker RETRY" is a ticker/record request.
- "How many scale-ins did I make?" maps to Category 8 execution/allocation language.
- "Should I stop retrying after a loss?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, fixed stable-instrument/account-local-entry-date
partitions, explicit/trusted period, declared gross or net basis/currency, and
complete Category 8 sequence coverage. Do not invent dates, tickers, or attempt scope.

### Required Data

- All candidate first-entry raw UTC/stable tie identities, partitions, states,
  Category 8 attempt ordinals, and barriers.
- Eligible repeat realised P/L, fees, currency, numerator/denominator, coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Validated result filters and privacy-safe attempt evidence references.
- Initial-attempt comparison when separately requested and compatible.

### Valid Filters

- Result filters after full partition/order/ordinal assignment only.
- No date/state/result/ticker filter may remove barriers or rebase attempts.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by instrument/non-overlapping period with identical Category 8
  ordinal and performance formula.
- Cross-account/day/instrument attempt sequences are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- First-entry UTC/stable-ID order; original ordinal `>= 2`; eligible P/L sum;
  qualifying count; exact division.
- Execution count, filter-first renumbering, or zero denominator as zero is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`,
  `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Fills/scale-ins treated as attempts; filtered renumbering; skipped barrier;
  mixed basis/currency; fee-incomplete net; invented date/context.
- Chasing/revenge diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret repeat attempts as Category 8 original ordinal `>= 2` zero-to-nonzero
lifecycles and performance as exact average selected-basis realised P/L. No
period or gross/net basis is invented. Zero count is unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: lifecycle attempt versus fill/scale-in when unclear,
then gross versus net basis, then exact period. Do not ask for motive.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Do you mean repeat zero-to-nonzero lifecycle attempts, not fills or scale-ins?"
2. If still needed: "Should performance use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing scope/basis/period/order; zero qualifying denominator; barrier-later
  partial sequence; fee-incomplete net; incompatible currency.
- Renumbering, private-ID disclosure, motive/causal claim, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic Category 8 repeat-ordinal average-P/L query over current
  read-only Journal, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Exact money per qualifying repeat attempt, numerator sum, denominator, one
  currency, gross or fee-complete net basis, ordinal/barrier coverage, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Gross uses accepted realised gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete evidence for
  every included repeat attempt in one currency; no fallback or zero substitution.

### Open-Trade Handling

- `legitimate_open` repeat retains ordinal/coverage but supplies no realised P/L.
- `needs_decision` and incomplete candidates are barriers; later results remain
  partial/unavailable without renumbering.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show numerator, qualifying repeat denominator, initial/open/decision/barrier coverage.
- Small repeat samples establish no chasing, persistence quality, cause, prediction,
  or advice; zero denominator is unavailable.

## `loss_chasing_proxy` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Recognize a descriptive user-declared proxy that combines only explicitly named
facts: Category 8 repeat/ordinal, Category 7 wait interval, Category 6 size,
and Category 2/3 selected-basis outcome or realised P/L. The user/trusted
specification must declare components, thresholds/comparators, Boolean
composition, fixed authorized account/instrument/local-day scope, and gross or
fee-complete net basis/currency where outcomes apply. Build complete pre-filter
lifecycle order and preserve all barriers. Report components and only a fully
declared deterministic qualifying count/rate with exact denominator. No complete
specification means not measurable. The proxy is not a diagnosis.

### Formal Wording

- "declared composite post-loss historical proxy over repeat, wait, size, and outcome facts"
- "non-diagnostic deterministic proxy predicate with explicit denominator"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I re-enter quickly and size up after the previous loss?"
- "Show my declared loss-chasing proxy, but just as historical facts."

### Trader Slang

- "Was I chasing losses?" maps only to a fully declared proxy and must state it
  cannot diagnose chasing, revenge, tilt, or emotion.
- "Revenge trades" is not a factual label unless the trader explicitly defined
  the proxy; ordinary post-loss activity alone is insufficient.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `LCP`, `chase proxy`, or `post-L combo` maps only in explicit declared-proxy
  context; bare `CHASE`, `L`, or ticker-like text is ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `loss chaseing proxy`
- `los chasing indicater`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL chase? fast bigger retry` lacks component thresholds, Boolean logic,
  basis, denominator, and period.
- `revenge after red` cannot use color or language alone to establish a loss or motive.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: "a loss-chasing proxy match" means one item satisfies the complete
  declared predicate.
- Plural: "loss-chasing proxy matches" means qualifying items under one unchanged
  specification, not diagnoses of multiple events.

### Full Questions

- "Under my declared rule, how many exact post-loss repeats were both under five
  minutes and larger than the predecessor?"
- "Return component facts, denominator, and unresolved coverage without diagnosing me."

### Commands

- "Apply only the declared repeat, wait, size, and selected-basis outcome predicates."
- "Keep every pre-filter barrier and label the output a proxy."

### Sentence Fragments

- "post-loss chase proxy"
- "quick bigger repeat after L"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Remove the size component" changes the declared predicate only; it requires
  recomputation and preserves period/basis/order.
- "Use net losses" changes outcome basis only with complete fees/currency.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant repeat plus short wait, not every trade after a loss."
- "Call it a proxy, not revenge trading or a diagnosis."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare proxy rate across two periods" requires identical component
  definitions, thresholds, Boolean logic, denominator, basis, and coverage.
- Differences remain descriptive and non-causal.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by proxy rate" remains Planned and requires identical
  specification, valid partitions, denominator counts, coverage, and tie rules.
- Partial/unavailable groups are not ranked as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "Proxy matches without size increase" is a different explicit Boolean
  predicate, not a reason to ignore size evidence silently.
- "Not revenge trading" preserves the proxy facts while rejecting diagnosis.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude current AAPL results" applies after complete sequence/component
  construction and cannot remove predecessor barriers.
- Excluding decision/open/flat predecessors never enables backward search.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net-loss repeats under five minutes with size ratio over 1.25 for selected
  AAPL days in August" requires every threshold, comparator, unit, basis,
  denominator, and coverage rule explicitly.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show count, rate, wait, size ratio, repeat ordinal, and barriers" returns a
  privacy-safe component bundle plus exact declared aggregation.
- "Tell me why" cannot add motive or causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Loss chasing" may mean a trader label, repeat attempts, quick timing, larger
  size, poor outcomes, or a psychological diagnosis.
- "Fast", "bigger", and "repeat" each require owning-category measure,
  threshold/comparator, unit, and scope.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker CHASE" is a ticker/record request.
- "What was time after previous loss?" maps to Category 7 interval language.
- "Diagnose why I revenge trade and tell me how to stop" is unsupported diagnosis/advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, fixed sequence partition, explicit/trusted period,
complete proxy specification, selected outcome basis/currency, compatible units,
and denominator. Do not invent components, thresholds, dates, ticker scope, or motive.

### Required Data

- Complete lifecycle order/barriers and Category 8 repeat facts.
- Any declared Category 7 wait, Category 6 size, and Category 2/3 outcome/P&L
  facts with units, fees, currency, thresholds, and applicability.
- Explicit Boolean composition, denominator, coverage, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe component evidence references and per-component breakdowns.
- Compatible declared count/rate comparisons across explicit periods.

### Valid Filters

- Validated current-result filters only after complete component sequence construction.
- No filter may delete predecessors/barriers or change component denominator silently.

### Valid Groupings

- Future instrument/period grouping only under identical complete specification.
- Mixed definitions, units, bases, denominators, or accounts are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Only explicitly declared component comparators, Boolean composition, distinct
  qualifying count, exact denominator, and division.
- No default OR/AND, threshold, motive score, or hidden component weighting.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `detect_pattern`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- `diagnose_performance` cannot turn the proxy into a psychological diagnosis.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Incomplete specification; mixed units/bases; skipped barriers; inferred loss,
  wait, size, or repeat component; hidden denominator; invented date/context.
- Diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, discipline score, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

No default proxy formula exists. Recognize the concept, preserve declared facts,
and return Planned/not measurable until components, thresholds/comparators,
Boolean composition, denominator, basis, scope, and period are explicit/trusted.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: component set first, then component threshold/
comparator, Boolean composition, denominator, outcome basis, then period. Never
ask the user to confirm a diagnosis.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which facts should your proxy combine: repeat attempt, wait time, size change, or outcome?"
2. If still needed: "What exact threshold and comparator should apply to the selected component?"
3. If still needed: "Should all selected conditions be required, or another explicit composition?"
4. If still needed: "What exact denominator should define the rate?"
5. If still needed: "Should loss use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
6. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing component specification, thresholds, Boolean logic, denominator,
  basis/unit/currency, period, or complete sequence coverage.
- Request to diagnose chasing/revenge/tilt, infer motive/cause, predict, expose
  private identifiers, or give trading advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic declared-proxy composer over owning-category read-only
  facts and coverage; no independent psychology model.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Declared component native units plus optional exact qualifying count or
  dimensionless rate/percentage, denominator, thresholds, basis/currency,
  sample/barrier coverage, and formula/specification version.

### Fee Handling

- Timing/repeat/quantity components are fee-independent.
- Outcome/P&L components require one gross or fee-complete net basis/currency;
  incomplete net never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Current `legitimate_open` may supply factual start/repeat/wait/size but not
  realised outcome.
- An exact predecessor open/decision/incomplete/flat/nonclassifiable for a
  required loss component is an unskippable barrier.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show component-qualified numerator, denominator, missing-component/barrier coverage.
- Any sample size remains a proxy only and proves no chasing, revenge, motive,
  causation, future behavior, or advice.

## `size_increase_after_losses` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed authorized account, stable instrument, and account-local
entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by first-entry raw
UTC then stable ID before filters. Select the exact immediate predecessor. It
must be `ready_closed` and a loss on one declared gross or fee-complete net
basis. Compare one explicitly selected Category 6 size measure for current and
predecessor in the same compatible unit/basis. Increase is strict
`current_size > predecessor_size`. Return both sizes, exact difference, and an
optional ratio only with nonzero predecessor denominator. No predecessor may
be skipped and no size measure, unit, aggregate, date, or motive is invented.

### Formal Wording

- "strict same-measure size increase relative to the exact selected-basis losing predecessor"
- "pre-filter predecessor-loss qualified current-minus-predecessor size comparison"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "Did I size up after the trade right before was a loss?"
- "Show how much larger my next trade was after losing."

### Trader Slang

- "Sized up after an L" maps to strict same-measure comparison with exact predecessor.
- "Doubled down after losing" may mean scale-in within one lifecycle and must not
  be forced into this cross-lifecycle concept or treated as motive.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `SIAL`, `size-up post-L`, or `SU after L` maps only in explicit lifecycle-size
  context; bare `SIZE`, `LOSS`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `size increse after losses`
- `sized up aftr lose`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL bigger after L?` needs the Category 6 size measure, outcome basis,
  period, and compatible unit.
- `2x after red` cannot use display color for loss or assume ratio denominator.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one current/exact-predecessor same-measure comparison.
- Plural: all qualifying current pairs without searching back past barriers.

### Full Questions

- "Was maximum position quantity strictly larger than the exact losing predecessor's?"
- "Show current size, predecessor size, difference, ratio, and barrier coverage."

### Commands

- "Select the exact predecessor before filters and compare the same Category 6 measure."
- "Do not mix shares/contracts/notional or skip an open/flat/unresolved predecessor."

### Sentence Fragments

- "size up after exact loss"
- "current minus prior-loss quantity"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use maximum position size" changes selected Category 6 measure only and
  requires it for both members.
- "Show percent" adds ratio/percentage only when predecessor size is nonzero.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant maximum held quantity, not shares per execution."
- "Use the immediately previous lifecycle; do not search for an earlier loss."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare size increases after losses versus wins" requires same size measure,
  unit/basis, outcome basis/currency, partitions, and coverage.
- Difference remains descriptive and non-causal.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by size increase after losses" remains Planned and requires an
  explicit aggregate/ranking value, same measure/unit, samples, coverage, and tie rules.
- Missing/zero denominators are unavailable, not zero-ranked.

### Negated Wording

- "Size increases, not decreases" selects strict positive difference.
- "Not scale-ins" preserves cross-lifecycle comparison grain.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude current AAPL results" filters outputs after exact predecessor selection.
- Excluding open/decision/flat predecessors never enables backward search.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Maximum-share size increases after net losses for selected long AAPL currents
  in August" requires same share unit and fee-complete loss basis.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show difference, ratio, count, and barriers" combines per-pair size facts
  with an explicitly requested aggregate/coverage.
- Asking why size increased cannot infer risk tolerance, chasing, or motive.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Size" may mean shares/contracts, maximum position, entry quantity, notional,
  or account exposure; no default measure exists.
- "After losses" may mean exact predecessor or any earlier loss; only exact
  immediate predecessor belongs here.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker SIZE" is a ticker/record request.
- "How many shares did I add during the losing trade?" is a Category 6/8 intra-lifecycle request.
- "Should I size down after losses?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorization, fixed partition, explicit/trusted period/current
population, Category 6 size measure/unit/basis for both members, selected
gross/net predecessor-loss basis/currency, and complete sequence coverage. Do
not invent normal size, date, ticker, or motive.

### Required Data

- Complete pre-filter order and exact predecessor state/outcome/fees/currency.
- Same accepted Category 6 measure for current/predecessor, compatible unit,
  exact values, nonzero ratio denominator where requested, and coverage.
- A future accepted named Category 9 query/result contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe pair evidence references and validated current-result filters.
- Explicit count/rate/average difference only when aggregate/denominator is declared.

### Valid Filters

- Current-result filters only after complete sequence/predecessor selection.
- No filter may change predecessor, size measure/unit, or remove barriers first.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping only with identical measure/unit/basis/outcome predicate/formula.
- Cross-unit/account/currency groups are invalid without accepted conversion facts.

### Valid Operators

- Exact predecessor; selected-basis loss; strict `current_size > predecessor_size`;
  same-unit difference; optional nonzero-denominator division.
- Implicit size measure, mixed units, clamped denominator, or any-earlier-loss search is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/mismatched size measure or unit; zero ratio denominator; skipped
  barrier; inferred loss; fee-incomplete net; invented date/context.
- Chasing/risk-tolerance diagnosis, motive, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

No size measure, unit, aggregate, period, or outcome basis is defaulted. Preserve
the exact predecessor and strict current-greater-than-predecessor comparison;
return Planned/unavailable until required fields resolve.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: Category 6 size measure first, then compatible unit,
gross versus net loss basis, requested per-pair versus aggregate output, then period.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which Category 6 size measure should I compare for both trades?"
2. If still needed: "What compatible unit should that size use?"
3. If still needed: "Should the exact predecessor loss use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
4. If still needed: "Do you want each pair, or an explicitly defined count/rate/summary?"
5. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing measure/unit/basis/period; exact predecessor non-ready/flat/non-loss;
  missing sizes; incompatible units; zero requested ratio denominator.
- Fee-incomplete net, skipped barrier, private-ID disclosure, motive/cause claim,
  prediction, or sizing advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic exact-predecessor Category 6 size-comparison query over
  current read-only Journal, outcome, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Selected Category 6 native unit for both sizes and difference; optional ratio/
  percentage only with nonzero predecessor; basis/currency, samples, barriers,
  and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Size arithmetic is fee-independent. Predecessor loss uses gross or fee-complete
  net P/L in one currency; incomplete net never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Current `legitimate_open` may qualify when its selected size fact is accepted.
- Exact predecessor must be `ready_closed` loss; open/decision/incomplete/flat/
  nonclassifiable predecessor is an unskippable barrier.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show pair count, selected measure/unit, difference/ratio denominator, fees,
  currency, and every barrier/missing-size class.
- Small samples establish no chasing, risk tolerance, cause, prediction, or advice.

## `shortened_wait_time_after_losses` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Within each fixed server-authorized account, stable instrument, and account-
local entry-date partition, order all current lifecycle candidates by first-
entry raw UTC then stable ID before filters. Select the exact immediate
predecessor. It must be `ready_closed` and a loss on one declared gross or
fee-complete net basis. Category 7 owns the exact positive raw-UTC interval
from predecessor final exit to current first entry. "Shortened" additionally
requires an explicit accepted baseline and comparator; it is true only when the
interval satisfies that declared shorter-than predicate. Return interval,
baseline, difference, and optional ratio only with nonzero baseline. No
predecessor, baseline, period, or recommended wait is invented.

### Formal Wording

- "exact previous-loss interval satisfying an explicit shorter-than baseline predicate"
- "pre-filter predecessor-loss final-exit-to-current-entry interval comparison"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "Did I wait less time after the trade right before was a loss?"
- "Show trades entered under five minutes after the exact previous loss."

### Trader Slang

- "Did I jump back in faster after an L?" requires an explicit comparison
  baseline/comparator and exact predecessor.
- "Revenge entry" or "too quick" never proves chasing, impatience, discipline,
  motive, causation, or an ideal wait.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `SWTAL`, `fast post-L`, or `under 5m after L` maps only with explicit interval
  context; bare `L`, `FAST`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `shortend wait after losses`
- `shorter wiat aftr lose`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL fast after L <5m?` needs outcome basis and trusted period/current scope;
  AAPL cannot reselect the predecessor.
- `quicker after red` lacks a factual loss basis and comparison baseline.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one current lifecycle's exact previous-loss interval versus baseline.
- Plural: multiple qualified intervals, each using its own exact predecessor and
  the same declared comparison contract.

### Full Questions

- "Which current trades began in less than five minutes after an exact gross-loss predecessor?"
- "Show interval, baseline, difference, count, and barrier coverage."

### Commands

- "Use Category 7's exact positive interval and compare it with the declared baseline."
- "Do not skip an open, flat, unresolved, or fee-nonclassifiable predecessor."

### Sentence Fragments

- "shorter wait after exact loss"
- "under 5m post-loss gap"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use ten minutes" changes baseline only; it does not change comparator,
  predecessor, basis, or period.
- "Show percent shorter" adds ratio only when baseline is nonzero.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant strictly under five minutes, not five minutes or less."
- "Use the immediately previous lifecycle, not any earlier loss."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare after-loss waits with my declared normal baseline" requires identical
  interval endpoints, comparator, outcome basis, timezone, samples, and coverage.
- Observed shorter waits remain descriptive and non-causal.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank tickers by shortest after-loss wait" remains Planned and requires exact
  intervals, same basis/scope, qualified counts, barriers, and tie policy.
- Missing/nonpositive/barrier intervals are unavailable, not zero-ranked.

### Negated Wording

- "Shortened waits, not longer waits" selects only intervals satisfying the
  declared shorter-than comparator.
- "Not cooldown violations" rejects rule/adherence interpretation.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude current AAPL trades" filters results after predecessor selection.
- Excluding open/decision/flat predecessors never enables searching farther back.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net-loss gaps under five minutes for selected long AAPL currents in August"
  applies current filters after complete order and requires fee-complete net loss.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show seconds, baseline, difference, ratio, sample count, and barriers" combines
  exact interval and declared comparison audit fields.
- "Tell me why I rushed" is unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Short wait" has no universal baseline or inclusive/exclusive comparator.
- "After losses" may mean exact predecessor, any earlier loss, or losing day;
  "loss" also requires gross or net basis.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker FAST" is a ticker/record request.
- "How long did the losing trade last?" maps to Category 7 hold duration.
- "How long should I wait after losing?" asks for trading advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorization, fixed account/instrument/local-day partition, explicit/
trusted current population and period, exact predecessor loss basis/currency,
accepted raw-UTC endpoints, account timezone, baseline, and comparator. Do not
invent a date, ticker, baseline, cooldown, or motive.

### Required Data

- Complete pre-filter candidate order, exact predecessor state/outcome/fees,
  predecessor final-exit UTC, current first-entry UTC, timezone, and coverage.
- Explicit baseline/comparator and future accepted named Category 9 query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe pair evidence references and interval display in seconds/minutes.
- Difference/ratio and explicit count/rate only with defined denominator.

### Valid Filters

- Current-result filters after complete order/exact predecessor selection only.
- No date/state/ticker/outcome filter may remove barriers or search for an earlier loss.

### Valid Groupings

- Future instrument/period grouping only with identical interval, baseline,
  comparator, outcome basis, and coverage rules.
- Mixed timezone/basis or rebuilt group-specific chains are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC subtraction, positive-gap test, declared shorter-than comparator,
  exact difference, and optional nonzero-baseline division.
- Clamping overlap, implicit baseline, or any-earlier-loss search is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `detect_pattern`,
  `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing baseline/comparator/basis/period; nonpositive interval; skipped barrier;
  fee-incomplete net; invented date/context.
- Revenge/impatience diagnosis, motive, cause, prediction, cooldown prescription, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use Category 7's exact positive interval after the exact immediate selected-
basis loss. No shortened baseline, comparator, period, or P/L basis is defaulted.
Return Planned/unavailable until required fields resolve.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: comparison baseline, inclusive versus strict
comparator, gross versus net loss basis, then period/current population.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "What exact wait-time baseline should define shortened?"
2. If still needed: "Should equality count, or only intervals strictly below it?"
3. If still needed: "Should the predecessor loss use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
4. If still needed: "What exact period or current-trade population should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing baseline/comparator/basis/period/endpoints; predecessor non-ready,
  flat/non-loss; current missing entry; overlap/nonpositive gap; zero ratio baseline.
- Fee-incomplete net, skipped barrier, private-ID request, motive/cause claim, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic Category 7 exact previous-loss interval comparison over
  current Journal, outcome, fee, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Exact positive seconds, baseline seconds, signed difference, optional ratio/
  percentage with nonzero baseline, outcome basis/currency, samples, barriers,
  and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Interval math is fee-independent. Predecessor loss uses gross or fee-complete
  net P/L in one currency; incomplete net never falls back to gross.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Current `legitimate_open` may qualify because its factual first entry exists.
- Exact predecessor must be `ready_closed` loss with final exit; open/decision/
  incomplete/flat/nonclassifiable predecessor is an unskippable barrier.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count, baseline/comparator, gap/overlap/barrier/fee coverage.
- Small samples establish no chasing, impatience, cause, prediction, or ideal wait.

## `profit_giveback` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Route recognized `profit_giveback` wording only to Category 10's single exact
candle calculation and its accepted selected-trade, direction, price, candle-
interval, sequence-limitation, provider-data, coverage, units, and formula
contract. Category 9 performs no candle, MFE/MAE, realised-P/L-path, daily-path,
or duplicate calculation. It may describe the authorized Category 10 result
without changing it. Until Category 10 is accepted/available, this route is
Planned/unavailable rather than approximated from Journal P/L.

### Formal Wording

- "Category 10 exact candle profit-giveback result"
- "routing reference to the sole versioned candle-path giveback calculation"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How much profit did this trade give back from its candle high?"
- "Show the Category 10 giveback result for the selected trade."

### Trader Slang

- "How much did I give back?" needs selected-trade and Category 10 context.
- "Round-tripped the winner" or "gave it all back" cannot establish motive,
  discipline, or a value without the Category 10 calculation.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `PG`, `giveback`, or `GB` maps only in explicit selected-trade candle context;
  bare `GB` or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `profit givebak`
- `gave bak profit`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `AAPL giveback?` needs a trusted selected trade and Category 10-required context;
  a ticker alone is not a selected lifecycle.
- `gave back 50%` may be a user claim, threshold, or request and cannot seed facts.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one Category 10 result for one authorized selected trade.
- Plural: multiple results only through Category 10's accepted multi-trade contract;
  Category 9 does not aggregate them independently.

### Full Questions

- "What is the exact Category 10 candle profit giveback for the selected trade?"
- "Show its interval and sequence limitations with coverage."

### Commands

- "Route this to Category 10 and preserve its exact result."
- "Do not substitute realised P/L, daily drawdown, or an invented candle peak."

### Sentence Fragments

- "selected trade profit giveback"
- "gave back from candle peak"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use the other selected trade" changes selected entity only through trusted context.
- "Show the limitations" requests Category 10 interval/coverage details, not a new calculation.

### Correction Wording

- "I meant candle profit giveback, not realised daily P/L."
- "Use Category 10's result; do not calculate another behavior metric."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare giveback for two selected trades" is valid only if Category 10
  supports compatible comparisons with identical definition/interval/coverage.
- Category 9 cannot normalize or recompute values.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank trades by giveback" remains Planned and requires Category 10-compatible
  results, sample/coverage limits, and deterministic tie rules.
- Missing candle results are unavailable, not zero-ranked.

### Negated Wording

- "Giveback, not daily loss" routes away from daily realised-path metrics.
- "Not MFE" distinguishes the requested Category 10 output without redefining either metric.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude trades without complete candles" may filter Category 10 results only
  while reporting missing-candle coverage.
- Category 9 cannot hide unavailable provider intervals.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Giveback for selected long AAPL trades in the declared period" is accepted
  only through Category 10's validated scope/filter contract; no period is invented.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show giveback, candle interval, sequence limitation, and coverage" routes all
  parts to Category 10.
- "Tell me why I gave it back" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Gave it back" may mean candle-path giveback, later realised losses, daily
  drawdown, profit-target continuation, or colloquial regret.
- A ticker or page alone does not identify an authorized selected trade unless trusted.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker GB" is a ticker/record request.
- "How much did daily P/L fall after the target?" is a realised-day path question.
- "Why am I bad at holding winners?" asks for diagnosis/motive.

### Context Requirements

Require Category 10's trusted authorized selected-trade scope and every required
trade/candle/provider fact. Do not invent trade selection, date, interval,
intrabar sequence, peak, or missing candle data.

### Required Data

- Exactly the authorized trade and versioned candle/provider facts required by
  Category 10, including interval and limitation coverage.
- Accepted Category 10 calculation/result contract and privacy-safe evidence references.

### Optional Data

- Category 10-supported comparison/ranking context and explanatory methodology.
- Display formatting that does not alter units or facts.

### Valid Filters

- Only filters accepted by Category 10 after authorization and selected-trade resolution.
- Category 9 introduces no independent ticker/date/direction/candle filters.

### Valid Groupings

- Only Category 10-supported compatible grouping.
- Category 9 defines no independent aggregate or duplicate group calculation.

### Valid Operators

- Only Category 10's exact versioned calculation and supported comparison operators.
- Journal-P/L approximation, invented peak, candle interpolation, or double count is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `analyze_trade`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `rank_results`, `explain_result`,
  and `inspect_data_quality` when Category 10 supports them.
- Every Category 9 route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- No selected trade; absent/incomplete Category 10 facts; mismatched interval/
  formula; duplicate behaviour or realised-path calculation.
- Motive, discipline diagnosis, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Interpret explicit "profit giveback" as a routing reference to Category 10 only.
No selected trade, date, interval, or value is defaulted. Return Planned/
unavailable when Category 10 cannot answer.

### Clarification Conditions

Use Category 10's clarification contract one field at a time. Resolve selected
trade first, then the next Category 10-required field such as interval/context.
Do not ask for a guessed peak or causal explanation.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which authorized trade should I use for the Category 10 giveback result?"
2. If still needed: "Which available Category 10 candle interval should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing selected trade, required trade prices/times/direction, candle/provider
  facts, accepted interval, or Category 10 capability.
- Request for duplicate realised-path calculation, invented intrabar sequence,
  private identifiers, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned routing to the Category 10 exact candle `profit_giveback` capability.
- Category 9 has no independent analytics tool, metric runtime, or AI Chat handler.

### Result Units

- Exactly Category 10's units, selected-trade scope, candle interval, data/
  sequence coverage, and formula version; Category 9 adds no value/count/rate.

### Fee Handling

- Inherit Category 10's declared treatment exactly.
- Category 9 cannot substitute gross/net Journal P/L, invent fees, or change basis.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Inherit Category 10's exact open-trade/candle rules without broadening them.
- Journal open/decision/incomplete state remains visible context but cannot create a result.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Preserve Category 10 trade count, candle interval, missing-data, and sequence limitations.
- Any sample supports no motive, discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory claim.
