## `daily_loss_limit_violations` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and account-local day, require an applicable
saved rule version with nonnegative daily loss limit `L`, effective scope, one
selected gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L basis, and one currency.
The server selects the version effective for each historical local day from
saved scope and effective-time facts; a user does not choose or rewrite that
version, and a current/later version is never borrowed. Missing or conflicting
effective-version evidence makes the affected day partial/unavailable. Order
eligible `ready_closed` closing events by raw UTC then stable ID and form the
cumulative selected-basis realised-P/L path. The first cumulative value
`<= -L` is the breach; equality counts. A breached day contributes exactly one
violation day regardless of recovery or later breach points. End-of-day P/L is
not a substitute. Missing/inapplicable rule or incomplete path is explicit
unavailable/partial coverage.

### Formal Wording

- "inclusive first cumulative realised-P/L breach of effective daily loss limit"
- "one violation-day count from ordered account-local close path"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How many days hit or crossed my saved daily loss limit?"
- "Did my cumulative net P/L breach the limit during the day, even if I recovered?"

### Trader Slang

- "How often did I hit max loss?" maps only to the applicable saved rule/path.
- "Blew my daily stop" is not proof of discipline, intent, or cause and may be
  confused with continued trading after stop.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `DLLV`, `max-loss breach`, or `daily L` maps only in explicit saved-rule
  context; bare `LOSS`, `DLL`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `daily los limit violatons`
- `max loss day brech`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `hit -500 net?` needs saved rule version, currency, effective day/period, and
  fee-complete net path.
- `AAPL max loss` may be a ticker/trade loss request, not an account-day rule breach.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one applicable account-local day with one first breach state.
- Plural: violation days count days once each, not every below-limit close point.

### Full Questions

- "On which declared days did cumulative gross P/L first become less than or equal to -L?"
- "Show violation-day count, first-breach time, rule version, and path coverage."

### Commands

- "Order ready-closed closing events and stop at the first inclusive breach per day."
- "Do not use end-of-day-only P/L or count repeated breach points."

### Sentence Fragments

- "daily loss limit breach days"
- "first cumulative <= -L"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes path basis only with fee-complete same-currency events.
- "Include equality" confirms canonical `<= -L`; it does not change rule version.

### Correction Wording

- "Count one violation day, not each close below the limit."
- "Use the rule effective that day, not my current limit."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare violation days across two periods" requires effective rule versions,
  same basis/currency semantics, applicable-day counts, and path coverage.
- Different limits must be shown; rates are not compared as if thresholds matched.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank months by violation days" remains Planned and requires explicit periods,
  effective rules, applicable-day denominators, coverage, and ties.
- Missing path/rule days are unavailable, not non-violation zeros.

### Negated Wording

- "Days without a violation" means complete applicable days with no cumulative
  `<= -L` point; partial days remain coverage.
- "Not end-of-day losses" preserves the intraday cumulative path.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude incomplete days" may keep them outside complete counts only while
  reporting their exact coverage.
- Excluding an unresolved close cannot repair the path or turn the day non-violating.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net daily-loss violations for the declared quarter" requires fee-complete
  one-currency paths and each day's effective saved rule.
- Ticker/side filters cannot silently redefine an account-level daily rule.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show count, applicable days, first breach times, limits, and incomplete paths"
  combines rule evaluation with coverage.
- "Tell me why I broke it" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Daily stop" may mean loss limit, continued-trading stop threshold, drawdown,
  or end-of-day loss.
- "Violation" requires effective rule version/applicability, basis, currency,
  and inclusive comparator; none is inferred.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show trades for the validated ticker LOSS" is a ticker/record request.
- "What was my worst losing day?" is a P/L ranking request.
- "What should my daily limit be?" asks for advice/settings guidance.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, explicit/trusted day or period, account IANA
timezone, applicable saved rule version/effective scope, `L`, selected basis,
currency, and complete ordered close path. Do not invent dates, rule values, or account scope.

### Required Data

- Effective saved rule/version/applicability and nonnegative `L` per local day.
- Eligible close raw UTC/stable IDs, realised P/L, fees, currency, timezone,
  lifecycle/decision coverage, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe first-breach evidence/time and complete applicable-day denominator.
- Separate gross/net audit display when each basis is independently complete.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted period over applicable local days.
- No ticker/side/outcome filter may redefine account daily path; no state filter
  may hide an event that affects coverage.

### Valid Groupings

- Future non-overlapping period grouping preserving each day's effective rule.
- Cross-account/currency paths and threshold-homogenized groups are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC/stable-ID close order, cumulative sum, inclusive `<= -L`, first-breach
  selection, one Boolean/count per day.
- End-of-day-only test, strict `<` substitution, repeated-point count, or P/L clamping is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `evaluate_rule`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/inapplicable rule; mixed basis/currency; incomplete net/path; invented
  date/limit; end-of-day substitution; repeated breaches counted.
- A saved rule whose comparator conflicts with canonical `<= -L` semantics
  cannot mutate this metric; return unavailable or route a separately defined
  rule/counterfactual capability.
- Discipline/motive diagnosis, causation, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use the applicable saved rule and canonical inclusive first-breach cumulative
path. The server resolves each historical day's effective version from saved
scope/effective-time facts; it never substitutes a current/later version or a
user-proposed rewrite. No rule identity, `L`, basis, currency, or date is
defaulted. The breach comparator is unconditionally cumulative selected-basis
P/L `<= -L`, with equality counted; an incompatible saved-rule comparator makes
this metric unavailable or routes to a separately defined rule/counterfactual.
Return Planned/unavailable for missing, conflicting, or incomplete evidence.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: rule identity only when genuinely ambiguous, then
gross versus net basis, then exact period. The server selects the historically
effective version; never ask the user which version should apply. Canonical
`<= -L` always counts equality and is not a clarification or user-editable choice.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. Only if rule identity is genuinely ambiguous: "Which saved daily-loss-limit rule do you mean?"
2. If still needed: "Should the cumulative path use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact day or period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing/inapplicable or conflicting effective rule version, `L`, period, timezone, basis/currency, eligible close
  order, or complete path; fee-incomplete net; zero applicable evidence.
- Saved-rule comparator semantics incompatible with canonical inclusive
  `<= -L`; this read-only metric cannot be redefined by a user response.
- Request to change the rule, expose private IDs, infer motive/cause, or recommend a limit.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic saved-rule cumulative-P/L first-breach query over
  read-only Journal, fee, timezone, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Boolean breach and 0/1 violation count per applicable local day; aggregate
  violation-day count only with explicit period/denominator, plus `L`, basis/
  currency, first-breach time, rule/formula versions, and coverage.

### Fee Handling

- Gross path uses accepted gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete same-currency
  events; incomplete net makes path partial/unavailable and never falls back.

### Open-Trade Handling

- `legitimate_open` has no realised close contribution; no unrealised P/L is invented.
- `needs_decision` or incomplete evidence affecting path makes the day partial/
  unavailable and cannot be skipped.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show violation-day count, applicable complete-day denominator, rule-version
  distribution, and missing-path coverage.
- Small samples support no discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory conclusion.

## `continued_trading_after_profit_target` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and account-local day, require an applicable
saved rule version with positive profit target `T`, effective scope, one
selected gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L basis, and one currency.
The server selects the version effective for each historical local day from
saved scope and effective-time facts; it never borrows a current/later version
or accepts a user rewrite. Missing or conflicting effective-version evidence
makes that day partial/unavailable. Order
eligible `ready_closed` closing events by raw UTC then stable ID, form the
cumulative selected-basis realised-P/L path, and select its first value `>= T`;
equality counts. After that attainment event, count later factual zero-to-
nonzero lifecycle starts in the same local day, ordered by raw UTC then stable
ID. Count qualifying starts from `ready_closed` and factually
`legitimate_open` lifecycles, not fills, scale-ins, exits, or the target-closing
event. Decision/incomplete evidence makes affected path or later sequence
partial/unavailable and is never skipped.

### Formal Wording

- "later lifecycle-start count after inclusive first cumulative profit-target attainment"
- "saved-rule continuation after cumulative realised P/L first reaches `T`"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How many new trades did I start after reaching my saved profit target?"
- "Did I open another trade after cumulative net P/L first hit the target?"

### Trader Slang

- "Did I keep trading after goal?" maps to later zero-to-nonzero starts only.
- "Kept pushing after green" is not proof of greed, discipline, motive, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `PT`, `daily PT`, or `after target` maps only with explicit saved-rule context;
  bare `PT`, `TGT`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `contined trading after profet target`
- `trades afer daily targt`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `hit 500 then more?` needs saved rule/version, currency, basis, and exact day/period.
- `TGT after target` may be ticker text rather than a saved profit-target request.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one later factual lifecycle start after first target attainment.
- Plural: later-start count across complete applicable account-local days.

### Full Questions

- "After cumulative gross P/L first became greater than or equal to T, how many later trades began that day?"
- "Show target-attainment time, later lifecycle starts, rule version, and coverage."

### Commands

- "Find first inclusive target attainment, then count later zero-to-nonzero starts."
- "Do not count scale-ins, fills, exits, or the closing event that reached target."

### Sentence Fragments

- "new trades after profit target"
- "first cumulative >= T then later starts"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes basis only when the full same-currency path is fee-complete.
- "Only later trades" confirms lifecycle-start grain; it does not remove open/decision coverage.

### Correction Wording

- "Use the rule version effective that day, not today's target."
- "Count new lifecycle starts, not every execution after the target."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare later-start counts after target by month" requires identical event
  grain, declared rule versions/thresholds, complete applicable days, and coverage.
- Different targets must remain visible and cannot be treated as equivalent exposure.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank months by starts after target" remains Planned and requires explicit
  periods, applicable-day denominators, thresholds, coverage, and tie handling.
- Partial days cannot rank as zero later activity.

### Negated Wording

- "No trading after target" means no later factual lifecycle start on a complete
  applicable day after first attainment; partial evidence is not zero.
- It does not mean there were no later fills or exits in an existing lifecycle.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude incomplete days" may remove them only from complete counts while
  preserving their coverage count and reason.
- A decision candidate cannot be skipped to claim a clean later sequence.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net starts after target in the declared quarter" requires fee-complete
  one-currency paths, each effective saved rule, and complete lifecycle order.
- Ticker/side filters cannot silently redefine an account-level daily target path.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show first attainment, later starts, thresholds, applicable days, and partial coverage"
  combines path evaluation, lifecycle sequencing, and coverage.
- "Explain why I kept trading" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "After target" may mean after a price target, trade exit, daily profit target,
  or end-of-day profit.
- "Continued trading" may mean executions or new lifecycles; this concept uses
  only later zero-to-nonzero lifecycle starts.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show TGT trades after noon" is a ticker/time filter request.
- "What was my profit after the target trade?" is a P/L request.
- "Should I stop trading when green?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, explicit/trusted day or period, account IANA
timezone, applicable saved rule/version/effective scope, positive `T`, selected
basis/currency, complete ordered close path, and later lifecycle sequence. Do
not invent dates, target, account, rule, or behavioural meaning.

### Required Data

- Effective saved rule/version/applicability and positive `T` per local day.
- Eligible close raw UTC/stable IDs, selected-basis realised P/L, fees/currency,
  lifecycle-start UTC/stable IDs/state, timezone, decision coverage, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe first-attainment evidence/time and later qualifying start evidence.
- Complete applicable-day denominator and separate gross/net audit display when complete.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted period over applicable local days.
- No ticker/side/outcome/state filter may alter the account path or hide a
  candidate affecting first attainment or later-start coverage.

### Valid Groupings

- Future non-overlapping period grouping preserving each day's rule and account.
- Cross-account/currency paths and threshold-homogenized groups are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC/stable-ID close order, cumulative sum, inclusive `>= T`, first-
  attainment selection, later raw-UTC lifecycle-start order, and count.
- End-of-day substitution, strict `>` substitution, execution count, or skipped barriers is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `evaluate_rule`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/inapplicable rule; mixed basis/currency; incomplete path or later
  sequence; invented date/target; end-of-day substitution; execution-grain count.
- Treating continuation as a rule violation without explicit rule semantics, or inferring motive/cause/advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use the applicable saved rule, inclusive first-attainment cumulative path, and
later lifecycle-start grain. The server resolves each historical day's
effective version from saved scope/effective-time facts and never substitutes
a current/later version or user-proposed rewrite. No rule identity, `T`, basis,
currency, date, or rule-violation meaning is defaulted. Missing or conflicting evidence returns Planned/unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: rule identity only when genuinely ambiguous, gross
versus net basis, then exact day/period. The server selects the historically
effective version; never ask the user which version should apply. Equality and
lifecycle-start grain are canonical under the saved rule.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. Only if rule identity is genuinely ambiguous: "Which saved profit-target rule do you mean?"
2. If still needed: "Should the cumulative path use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact day or period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing/inapplicable or conflicting effective rule version, `T`, period, timezone, basis/currency, close order,
  lifecycle starts, or complete coverage; fee-incomplete net; zero applicable evidence.
- Request to expose private IDs, diagnose motive, assert causation, predict, or advise.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic saved-rule cumulative-P/L first-attainment plus later-
  lifecycle-start query over read-only Journal, rule, fee, timezone, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Later lifecycle-start count per applicable local day and aggregate count only
  with explicit complete-day denominator, plus target, basis/currency, first-
  attainment time, rule/formula versions, and coverage.

### Fee Handling

- Gross path uses accepted gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete same-currency
  events; incomplete net makes path partial/unavailable and never falls back.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle may count as a later start from its
  factual entry but contributes no realised close P/L.
- `needs_decision` or incomplete evidence affecting either sequence makes the
  affected day partial/unavailable and cannot be skipped.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show later-start count, complete applicable-day denominator, rule/threshold
  distribution, and path/lifecycle coverage.
- Any sample supports no motive, causal, predictive, discipline, or advisory conclusion.

## `continued_trading_after_stop_threshold` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and account-local day, require an applicable
saved rule version with nonnegative loss-stop threshold `L`, effective scope,
one selected gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L basis, and one currency.
The server selects the version effective for each historical local day from
saved scope and effective-time facts; it never borrows a current/later version
or accepts a user rewrite. Missing or conflicting effective-version evidence
makes that day partial/unavailable.
Order eligible `ready_closed` closing events by raw UTC then stable ID, form the
cumulative selected-basis realised-P/L path, and select its first value
`<= -L`; equality counts. After that breach event, count later factual zero-to-
nonzero lifecycle starts in the same local day, ordered by raw UTC then stable
ID. Count starts from `ready_closed` and factually `legitimate_open` lifecycles,
not fills, scale-ins, exits, or the stop-breaching close. Decision/incomplete
evidence makes the affected path or later sequence partial/unavailable and is never skipped.

### Formal Wording

- "later lifecycle-start count after inclusive first cumulative stop-threshold breach"
- "saved-rule continuation after cumulative realised P/L first reaches `-L`"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How many new trades did I start after hitting my saved loss stop?"
- "Did I open another trade after cumulative net P/L first crossed the stop?"

### Trader Slang

- "Did I keep trading after max loss?" maps to later zero-to-nonzero starts only.
- "Chased after stopping out" does not establish revenge, motive, discipline, or cause.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `SL`, `daily stop`, or `after max loss` maps only with explicit saved-rule
  context; bare `STOP`, `SL`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `contined trading after stop thresold`
- `trades afer max los`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `below -500 then more?` needs saved rule/version, currency, basis, and exact day/period.
- `LOSS after stop` may be ticker text or a stopped trade, not an account-day rule request.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one later factual lifecycle start after first stop breach.
- Plural: later-start count across complete applicable account-local days.

### Full Questions

- "After cumulative gross P/L first became less than or equal to -L, how many later trades began that day?"
- "Show stop-breach time, later lifecycle starts, rule version, and coverage."

### Commands

- "Find first inclusive stop breach, then count later zero-to-nonzero starts."
- "Do not count scale-ins, fills, exits, or the close that breached the stop."

### Sentence Fragments

- "new trades after loss stop"
- "first cumulative <= -L then later starts"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" changes basis only when the full same-currency path is fee-complete.
- "Only new trades" confirms lifecycle-start grain; it does not remove open/decision coverage.

### Correction Wording

- "Use the stop rule effective that day, not today's setting."
- "Count new lifecycle starts, not every execution after the breach."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare later-start counts after stop by month" requires identical event
  grain, declared rule versions/thresholds, complete applicable days, and coverage.
- Different stops must remain visible and cannot be treated as equivalent exposure.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank months by starts after stop" remains Planned and requires explicit
  periods, applicable-day denominators, thresholds, coverage, and tie handling.
- Partial days cannot rank as zero later activity.

### Negated Wording

- "No trading after stop" means no later factual lifecycle start on a complete
  applicable day after first breach; partial evidence is not zero.
- It does not mean there were no later fills or exits in an existing lifecycle.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude incomplete days" may remove them only from complete counts while
  preserving their coverage count and reason.
- A decision candidate cannot be skipped to claim a clean later sequence.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net starts after stop in the declared quarter" requires fee-complete one-
  currency paths, each effective saved rule, and complete lifecycle order.
- Ticker/side filters cannot silently redefine an account-level daily stop path.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show first breach, later starts, thresholds, applicable days, and partial coverage"
  combines path evaluation, lifecycle sequencing, and coverage.
- "Explain why I traded again" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "After stop" may mean after a stop order, stopped trade, daily loss rule, or time cutoff.
- "Continued trading" may mean executions or new lifecycles; this concept uses
  only later zero-to-nonzero lifecycle starts.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show LOSS trades after noon" is a ticker/time filter request.
- "What was my P/L after a stop-order exit?" is a trade/P/L request.
- "What should my max loss be?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require server-authorized account, explicit/trusted day or period, account IANA
timezone, applicable saved rule/version/effective scope, nonnegative `L`,
selected basis/currency, complete ordered close path, and later lifecycle
sequence. Do not invent dates, stop, account, rule, or behavioural meaning.

### Required Data

- Effective saved rule/version/applicability and nonnegative `L` per local day.
- Eligible close raw UTC/stable IDs, selected-basis realised P/L, fees/currency,
  lifecycle-start UTC/stable IDs/state, timezone, decision coverage, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe first-breach evidence/time and later qualifying start evidence.
- Complete applicable-day denominator and separate gross/net audit display when complete.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted period over applicable local days.
- No ticker/side/outcome/state filter may alter the account path or hide a
  candidate affecting first breach or later-start coverage.

### Valid Groupings

- Future non-overlapping period grouping preserving each day's rule and account.
- Cross-account/currency paths and threshold-homogenized groups are invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw-UTC/stable-ID close order, cumulative sum, inclusive `<= -L`, first-
  breach selection, later raw-UTC lifecycle-start order, and count.
- End-of-day substitution, strict `<` substitution, execution count, or skipped barriers is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `evaluate_rule`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `analyze_sequence`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/inapplicable rule; mixed basis/currency; incomplete path or later
  sequence; invented date/stop; end-of-day substitution; execution-grain count.
- Treating later activity alone as a daily-limit/adherence violation, or inferring motive/cause/advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use the applicable saved rule, inclusive first-breach cumulative path, and later
lifecycle-start grain. The server resolves each historical day's effective
version from saved scope/effective-time facts and never substitutes a current/
later version or user-proposed rewrite. No rule identity, `L`, basis, currency,
date, daily-limit-violation meaning, or adherence meaning is defaulted. Missing
or conflicting evidence returns Planned/unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: rule identity only when genuinely ambiguous, gross
versus net basis, then exact day/period. The server selects the historically
effective version; never ask the user which version should apply. Equality and
lifecycle-start grain are canonical under the saved rule.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. Only if rule identity is genuinely ambiguous: "Which saved loss-stop rule do you mean?"
2. If still needed: "Should the cumulative path use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
3. If still needed: "What exact day or period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing/inapplicable or conflicting effective rule version, `L`, period, timezone, basis/currency, close order,
  lifecycle starts, or complete coverage; fee-incomplete net; zero applicable evidence.
- Request to expose private IDs, diagnose motive, assert causation, predict, or advise.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic saved-rule cumulative-P/L first-breach plus later-
  lifecycle-start query over read-only Journal, rule, fee, timezone, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Later lifecycle-start count per applicable local day and aggregate count only
  with explicit complete-day denominator, plus stop, basis/currency, first-
  breach time, rule/formula versions, and coverage.

### Fee Handling

- Gross path uses accepted gross P/L. Net requires fee-complete same-currency
  events; incomplete net makes path partial/unavailable and never falls back.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A factually `legitimate_open` lifecycle may count as a later start from its
  factual entry but contributes no realised close P/L.
- `needs_decision` or incomplete evidence affecting either sequence makes the
  affected day partial/unavailable and cannot be skipped.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show later-start count, complete applicable-day denominator, rule/threshold
  distribution, and path/lifecycle coverage.
- Any sample supports no motive, causal, predictive, discipline, or advisory conclusion.

## `time_cutoff_violations` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account, require an applicable saved cutoff-rule
version with effective scope, account IANA timezone, explicit governed event
grain, local cutoff, and explicit inclusive/exclusive prohibited-side
comparator. The server selects the version effective for each historical event
from saved scope and effective-time facts; it never borrows a current/later
version or accepts a user rewrite. Missing or conflicting effective-version
evidence is partial/unavailable. Convert each governed event's raw UTC instant
to account-local time and count each distinct applicable event once when it
satisfies that comparator.
There is no default grain or comparator; first lifecycle entry applies only
when the rule explicitly governs it. Missing rule, grain, comparator, time,
applicability, timezone, or event coverage is partial/unavailable.

### Formal Wording

- "distinct governed-event violations of the effective saved cutoff rule"
- "raw-UTC event instants compared in account-local time using the declared comparator"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How many events violated my saved time cutoff?"
- "Did any governed entries happen at or after my cutoff?"

### Trader Slang

- "How often did I trade late?" needs the rule's exact event grain and comparator.
- "After the bell" is not automatically a cutoff violation or discipline label.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `TCV`, `EOD cutoff`, or `late` maps only in explicit saved-rule context;
  bare `CUT`, `TIME`, or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `time cutof violatons`
- `late entrie breach`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `after 3:30?` needs rule/version, governed grain, comparator, timezone, and period.
- `TIME cutoff` may contain a ticker or generic time filter, not a saved-rule request.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one distinct applicable governed event satisfying the comparator.
- Plural: distinct violation-event count at the declared grain.

### Full Questions

- "Under the rule effective that day, which governed events violated its local cutoff?"
- "Show violation count, applicable-event denominator, comparator, timezone, and coverage."

### Commands

- "Use the saved event grain and comparator exactly."
- "Convert raw UTC through the account timezone before comparing with the cutoff."

### Sentence Fragments

- "cutoff violations"
- "entries at or after saved cutoff"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Equality counts" selects an inclusive comparator only if that matches the saved rule.
- "Use entries" requires a rule version explicitly governing lifecycle entry.

### Correction Wording

- "The rule governs order submissions, not fills."
- "Use strictly after, not at-or-after."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare cutoff-violation rates by month" requires the same declared grain,
  comparator semantics, applicable-event denominators, timezone, and coverage.
- Different rule versions/cutoffs remain visible rather than normalized away.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank periods by cutoff violations" remains Planned and requires explicit
  periods, applicable denominators, rule versions, coverage, and tie handling.
- Partial periods cannot rank as zero.

### Negated Wording

- "No cutoff violations" means no applicable governed event met the comparator
  in complete coverage; missing events are not compliant zeros.
- "Not entries" changes grain only when another saved-rule grain is explicit.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude missing-time events" may keep them out of determinate counts only
  while preserving their unavailable coverage.
- Filters cannot hide applicable governed events to improve adherence.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Submission cutoff violations last month" requires a saved rule governing
  submission events, explicit month, comparator, timezone, and complete coverage.
- Ticker/side filters are valid only if the saved rule's scope explicitly supports them.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show violating events, denominator, rule versions, cutoffs, and unknown coverage"
  combines rule evaluation and coverage.
- "Explain why I traded late" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Late trade" may mean entry, fill, close, submission, allocation, or lifecycle start.
- "After 4" needs timezone, local date/period, saved cutoff, and comparator.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Show TIME trades after 4" may be a ticker/time filter.
- "How did afternoon trades perform?" is time-of-day performance.
- "What cutoff should I use?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, saved rule/version/effective scope, explicit/trusted
period, account IANA timezone, governed event grain, cutoff, prohibited-side
comparator, raw UTC event instants, applicability, and coverage. Invent none.

### Required Data

- Saved rule/version/scope/applicability, governed grain, local cutoff, comparator, timezone.
- Stable governed-event IDs, raw UTC instants, lifecycle/decision state, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe event evidence and local converted instants.
- Applicable-event denominator and separate counts by rule version.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit/trusted period and rule-supported scope dimensions.
- No filter may silently change event grain or hide unknown/applicable events.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by non-overlapping period or explicit rule version while preserving grain.
- Cross-account/timezone or mixed-grain grouping is invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Raw UTC to account-local conversion, declared `>`/`>=`/`<`/`<=` comparison,
  stable-event deduplication, count, denominator, and optional rate.
- Default grain/comparator, server-time comparison, or execution/lifecycle substitution is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `evaluate_rule`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing/inapplicable rule, grain, comparator, timezone, event instant, or coverage.
- Generic time performance, inferred discipline/motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use only the applicable saved rule's grain, cutoff, and comparator. There is no
default grain or inclusive/exclusive choice. The server resolves each event's
historically effective version from saved scope/effective-time facts and never
substitutes a current/later version or user-proposed rewrite. After rule
identity is resolved, event grain and comparator come only from that effective
saved rule. Missing or conflicting stored grain/comparator facts make the
result partial/unavailable; the read-only response directs the trader to
correct or version the saved rule outside the query rather than accepting replacement facts.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: rule identity only when genuinely ambiguous, then
exact period separately if needed. The server selects the historically
effective version and reads its stored event grain and comparator; never ask
the user to choose a version, grain, or equality behavior for this read-only query.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. Only if rule identity is genuinely ambiguous: "Which saved cutoff rule do you mean?"
2. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing, inapplicable, or conflicting effective rule version; missing or
  conflicting stored event grain/comparator; missing event/timezone facts;
  unknown applicability; incomplete coverage; or zero denominator.
- A user response cannot replace missing/conflicting rule facts; direct the
  trader to correct or version the saved rule outside this read-only query.
- Request for private identifiers, motive/cause diagnosis, prediction, or cutoff advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned deterministic saved-rule governed-event time-comparison query over
  read-only rule, Journal, timezone, applicability, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Nonnegative distinct violation-event count at declared grain; optional
  dimensionless rate over all applicable governed events, with zero denominator
  unavailable; include rule version, cutoff, comparator, timezone, and coverage.

### Fee Handling

- Event-time comparison is fee-independent.
- Any explicitly composed P/L predicate must preserve its gross or fee-complete net basis/currency.

### Open-Trade Handling

- Governed factual events from `ready_closed` or factually `legitimate_open`
  lifecycles may qualify only when the rule applies to that grain.
- Decision/incomplete/missing-time/unknown-applicability evidence remains partial/unavailable, never compliant by default.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show violations, applicable-event denominator, rule-version distribution, and unknown coverage.
- Any sample supports no motive, discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory claim.

## `rule_adherence` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit
saved rule version and stable decision/opportunity grain. Preserve every
candidate's effective scope and applicability before analytic filters. Each
applicable decision needs an explicit factual `followed` or `broken`
determination from the trader with provenance or from a separately accepted
machine-verifiable predicate for that exact rule version. The denominator is
all distinct applicable decisions; rate is followed count divided by it.
Unknown determinations remain denominator/coverage and make the rate partial or
unavailable. Zero denominator is unavailable.

### Formal Wording

- "versioned applicable-decision followed/broken determination rate"
- "followed count divided by all distinct applicable decisions"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I follow this saved rule?"
- "Show followed, broken, and applicable decisions with unknown determinations."

### Trader Slang

- "Did I stick to my rules?" needs a specific rule/version and applicable grain.
- "Good discipline" is not a factual rule-adherence label.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `RA` or `rule %` maps only in explicit rule-review context; ticker-like `RULE`
  or bare `followed` remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `rule adherance`
- `folowed vs brokn`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `followed it 80%?` needs rule/version, period, decision grain, and applicability.
- `RULE wins` is a ticker/outcome request, not adherence evidence.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one applicable decision with factual followed/broken determination.
- Plural: deduplicated followed/broken counts and rate over applicable decisions.

### Full Questions

- "For the declared rule version, what were followed, broken, and unknown applicable decisions?"
- "What is the adherence rate with denominator and coverage?"

### Commands

- "Use only explicit applicability and factual followed/broken determinations."
- "Keep unknown applicable decisions in denominator coverage."

### Sentence Fragments

- "rule adherence rate"
- "followed vs broken decisions"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Only this rule" narrows to its explicit version/effective scope.
- "Include unknown determinations" preserves them in denominator/coverage.

### Correction Wording

- "That threshold event is not automatically a general rule break."
- "Use the rule version effective then, not the current version."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare adherence by month" requires the same rule/grain semantics or visible
  version splits, applicable denominators, unknown coverage, and periods.
- Performance differences do not explain adherence differences.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank rules by adherence rate" remains Planned and requires explicit rules,
  comparable grains, denominators, unknown coverage, and ties.
- Zero-applicability rules are unavailable, not zero percent.

### Negated Wording

- "Did not follow" maps only to an explicit factual `broken` determination.
- Unknown, inapplicable, losing, or open does not mean broken.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude inapplicable decisions" follows factual applicability.
- Unknown applicable determinations cannot be silently excluded from denominator coverage.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Adherence to rule version 3 last month" requires exact effective scope,
  decision grain, applicability, determinations, and coverage.
- Outcome/ticker filters cannot redefine the rule's candidate population unless its scope allows them.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show followed, broken, denominator, unknowns, and version changes" combines counts and coverage.
- "Tell me why I broke it" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "My rules" does not identify a saved rule/version or grain.
- "Adherence" may mean rule, setup, or plan adherence and needs the authored object.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "How many daily-loss violations?" is a specific threshold metric.
- "Did my winners follow the plan?" lacks factual determinations.
- "How can I become disciplined?" asks for coaching/advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, explicit/trusted period, saved rule/version/effective
scope, stable decision grain, full pre-filter candidate population, factual
applicability, followed/broken/unknown determination, provenance, and coverage.

### Required Data

- Rule/version/scope and stable candidate decision IDs with applicability.
- Trader/system factual determination provenance or accepted exact predicate, plus future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe determination evidence and separate counts by rule version.
- Explicit machine-predicate version when applicable.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit period and dimensions supported by the saved rule's effective scope.
- No outcome/state filter may remove applicable unknowns or redefine denominator.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by non-overlapping period or explicit rule version/grain.
- Cross-account or incompatible decision-grain aggregation is invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Stable-decision deduplication, applicability selection, factual-state counts,
  denominator, division, and unknown coverage; zero denominator unavailable.
- Outcome inference, missing-state coercion, or post-filter denominator construction is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intents: `evaluate_rule`, `calculate_metric`,
  `summarize_performance`, `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`,
  `rank_results`, `explain_result`, and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing rule/version/grain/applicability/determination provenance; zero denominator; hidden unknowns.
- Inference from outcome, size, timing, notes, or personality; motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use the named saved rule/version and its complete applicable decision
population. Do not default a rule, infer followed/broken, or treat unknown as
compliant. Missing facts return Planned/unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: saved rule/version, exact period, then decision
grain if the rule contract does not identify it.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which saved rule and version should I evaluate?"
2. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"
3. If still needed: "What decision or opportunity grain does the rule apply to?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing saved rule/version, applicability, factual determination/provenance,
  candidate coverage, or nonzero denominator.
- Request for private identifiers, discipline diagnosis, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned versioned applicable-decision adherence query over read-only saved-rule,
  trader-review/accepted-predicate, authorization, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Followed count, broken count, all-applicable-decision denominator, and
  dimensionless rate/percentage with unknown coverage, rule version, grain,
  period, account scope, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Not applicable to nonfinancial rules.
- An exact P/L predicate must declare gross or fee-complete net basis/currency;
  incomplete net yields unknown rather than gross fallback.

### Open-Trade Handling

- An open-associated applicable decision counts only with factual applicability
  and followed/broken determination.
- Open/decision/incomplete state never implies either label; missing determination remains denominator/coverage.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show counts, applicable denominator, unknown coverage, and rule-version distribution.
- Small samples support no character, discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory claim.

## `mistake_frequency` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit
versioned trader-authored mistake taxonomy/label, a stable decision/trade/review
grain, and factual applicability for every in-scope item. An applicable item
with at least one factual trader-applied label counts once in the overall
mistake-item numerator; label-kind counts remain separate. The denominator is
all distinct applicable items and frequency is numerator divided by it. Unknown
label determinations remain denominator/coverage and make the rate partial or
unavailable. Zero denominator is unavailable. Losses, rule breaks, results,
proxies, and system guesses never create mistake labels.

### Formal Wording

- "distinct trader-labelled mistake-item frequency under a versioned taxonomy"
- "mistake-item count divided by all distinct applicable items"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I label a trade as a mistake?"
- "Show mistake-labelled items and applicable items with unknown determinations."

### Trader Slang

- "How many screwups did I tag?" maps only to explicit trader labels.
- "Bad trades" does not automatically mean mistakes.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `MF`, `mistake %`, or `error tags` maps only with explicit taxonomy context;
  ticker-like `ERR` or `BAD` remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `mistake frequncy`
- `misstake lables`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `how many mistakes?` needs taxonomy/version, grain, period, and applicability.
- `ERR losses` may be ticker/outcome text, not trader-labelled mistakes.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one distinct applicable item with at least one explicit mistake label.
- Plural: deduplicated mistake-item count and rate, with label-kind counts separate.

### Full Questions

- "Under taxonomy version 2, what fraction of applicable trades did I label as mistakes?"
- "Show mistake items, denominator, unknown labels, and coverage."

### Commands

- "Count each labelled item once overall, even with multiple mistake labels."
- "Do not infer mistakes from losses or rule events."

### Sentence Fragments

- "mistake frequency"
- "tagged mistake trades"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Break down by label" adds separate label-kind counts without changing overall deduplication.
- "Include unknown determinations" preserves applicable unknowns in denominator/coverage.

### Correction Wording

- "Count items, not total label instances."
- "Use my taxonomy version effective then, not a new system label."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare mistake frequency by month" requires the same taxonomy/grain or
  visible versions, applicable denominators, unknown coverage, and periods.
- Outcome differences do not explain labelled-frequency differences.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank mistake labels by count" is a separate label-kind breakdown and remains Planned.
- "Rank months by mistake rate" requires denominators, versions, coverage, and ties.

### Negated Wording

- "No mistake" requires an explicit reviewed applicable item with no label under
  the declared contract; unknown is not no-mistake.
- Inapplicable items are not clean examples.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude inapplicable reviews" follows explicit applicability.
- Applicable unknown labels cannot be silently removed from denominator coverage.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Entry mistakes on day trades last month" requires explicit taxonomy label,
  stable grain, applicability, period, and complete review coverage.
- Loss/win filters cannot create or erase labels.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show frequency, label breakdown, denominator, unknowns, and taxonomy versions"
  combines deduplication and coverage.
- "Explain why I make mistakes" adds unsupported motive/causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Mistakes" may mean trader labels, rule breaks, losses, or general review notes.
- "Frequency" needs a declared item grain and applicable denominator.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "How often did I lose?" is loss frequency.
- "Count rule violations" is rule evaluation.
- "How do I stop making mistakes?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, explicit/trusted period, trader-authored taxonomy/
label version, stable item grain, full candidate population, explicit
applicability, label/unknown state, provenance, and coverage. Invent none.

### Required Data

- Taxonomy/version, stable authorized item IDs/grain, applicability, trader label state/provenance.
- Review/decision coverage and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe label notes/evidence and separate label-kind breakdown.
- Explicit taxonomy effective dates and reviewer identity class without private identity values.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit period and taxonomy-defined label/scope dimensions.
- No outcome/state filter may infer labels or hide applicable unknowns.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by non-overlapping period, taxonomy version, or declared label kind.
- Cross-account or incompatible item-grain aggregation is invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Stable-item deduplication, applicability selection, any-explicit-label Boolean,
  count, denominator, division, label-kind counts, and unknown coverage.
- Label-instance numerator, system classification, or zero-denominator rate is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intent `evaluate_label` selects and evaluates the explicit
  trader-labelled population first. After that population is fixed, compatible
  intents are `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `explain_result`,
  and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing taxonomy/version/grain/applicability/label provenance; zero denominator; hidden unknowns.
- Mistake inference from outcome/rule/proxy, personality diagnosis, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Use only explicit trader-applied labels under the named version and full
applicable item population. Do not default taxonomy/grain or infer clean/
mistake state. Missing facts return Planned/unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: taxonomy/label version, item grain, then exact period.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which trader-authored mistake taxonomy or label version should I use?"
2. If still needed: "Should the applicable item grain be decisions, trades, or reviews?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing explicit label/version, item grain, applicability, review coverage, or nonzero denominator.
- Request for private notes/identity, inferred diagnosis, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned trader-labelled distinct-item frequency query over read-only review,
  taxonomy, authorization, applicability, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Nonnegative distinct mistake-item count, all-applicable-item denominator,
  dimensionless rate/percentage, optional separate label-kind counts, unknown
  coverage, taxonomy version, grain, period, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Not applicable; labels/frequency cannot be inferred from gross/net P/L or fees.
- Any separate performance query retains its own basis, currency, and coverage.

### Open-Trade Handling

- An applicable factual open item counts only when explicitly trader-labelled under the version.
- Open/decision/incomplete/outcome state creates no label; unknown determination remains coverage.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show labelled-item count, applicable denominator, unknown coverage, and taxonomy versions.
- Any sample supports no carelessness, discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory claim.

## `mistake_cost` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

Require an explicit versioned trader-authored mistake label/applicability fact
linked to authorized stable evidence. The contract-defined counterfactual
difference additionally requires a separately approved, versioned historical
simulation/comparison contract supplying an authorized no-mistake comparison
result for the same item, identical gross or fee-complete net realised-P/L
basis, and same currency. The item difference is approved counterfactual P/L
minus factual P/L; compatible item differences may be summed with qualifying
count and missing-counterfactual coverage. It is a method-defined historical
comparison, not observed truth or proof that the labelled mistake caused the
difference. Without the approved contract and result, `mistake_cost` is
unavailable. Factual P/L on labelled mistakes is a separate descriptive result
and is never the contract-defined counterfactual difference.

### Formal Wording

- "approved counterfactual selected-basis P/L minus factual selected-basis P/L"
- "method-defined historical comparison for explicit versioned trader-labelled mistakes"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "What did my labelled mistakes cost under the approved comparison method?"
- "If no approved counterfactual exists, show that mistake cost is unavailable."

### Trader Slang

- "How much did my mistakes cost me?" requires explicit labels and approved counterfactuals.
- "Money lost on bad trades" is factual result, not the contract-defined counterfactual difference.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `MC`, `error cost`, or `mistake $` maps only with label and counterfactual context;
  bare `COST` or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `mistake cosst`
- `cost of misstakes`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `mistakes cost 2k?` needs label/version, approved counterfactual method, basis, currency, and period.
- `COST losses` may be ticker/outcome text, not a counterfactual request.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one qualifying labelled item with compatible factual and approved counterfactual results.
- Plural: exact sum across compatible qualifying items with missing-counterfactual coverage.

### Full Questions

- "Using the approved method version, what is counterfactual minus factual net P/L for each labelled mistake?"
- "Show aggregate cost, qualifying count, missing counterfactuals, fees, and currency."

### Commands

- "Return unavailable when no approved counterfactual exists."
- "Keep factual P/L on labelled mistakes separate from the contract-defined counterfactual difference."

### Sentence Fragments

- "mistake cost"
- "labelled mistake counterfactual difference"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Use net" requires fee-complete identical net treatment on both sides.
- "Just show actual P/L" changes to descriptive result on labelled mistakes, not `mistake_cost`.

### Correction Wording

- "Do not call the labelled trades' factual losses mistake cost."
- "Use the approved counterfactual method version and same currency."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare mistake cost by month" requires compatible taxonomy and
  counterfactual versions, identical basis/currency, qualifying counts, and coverage.
- Missing counterfactuals cannot be treated as zero cost.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank mistake types by cost" remains Planned and requires compatible explicit
  labels, approved counterfactuals, bases/currencies, coverage, and ties.
- Factual P/L rankings must be labelled as result on mistakes instead.

### Negated Wording

- "No mistake cost" means an approved comparison yields zero, not that the
  counterfactual is missing.
- Unlabelled/inapplicable items do not prove zero cost.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude missing counterfactuals" may restrict qualifying sums only while
  reporting the excluded coverage and denominator.
- Excluding adverse factual results to reduce cost is invalid.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "Net entry-mistake cost last quarter" requires explicit label version,
  approved method version, fee-complete same-currency pairs, period, and coverage.
- Outcome filters cannot create mistake labels or counterfactual values.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show item costs, total, qualifying count, missing methods, basis, and coverage"
  combines counterfactual comparison and quality reporting.
- "Tell me why mistakes caused losses" adds unsupported causation.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Cost" may mean factual P/L, fees, opportunity cost, or approved counterfactual difference.
- "Mistakes" needs an explicit trader-authored label/version and applicability fact.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Sum P/L for mistake-tagged trades" is descriptive factual result on labels.
- "How much did fees cost?" is fee analysis.
- "What would I have made with perfect entries?" requests an invented counterfactual.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized stable item, explicit trader label/version/applicability,
approved counterfactual contract/result/provenance/version, factual realized
P/L, identical selected basis and currency, fee coverage, period, and evidence coverage.

### Required Data

- Trader-authored label/version/applicability and stable authorized item identity.
- Factual P/L plus approved no-mistake counterfactual P/L/method version in compatible basis/currency.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe method/evidence references and separate factual result-on-mistakes report.
- Item-level differences, qualifying denominator, and missing-counterfactual reason counts.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit period and taxonomy/method-supported label dimensions.
- No filter may invent a counterfactual, hide missing coverage, or mix bases/currencies.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by non-overlapping period or explicit label kind with compatible method/basis/currency.
- Cross-currency, mixed-method, or mixed-grain sums are invalid without an accepted conversion contract.

### Valid Operators

- Same-item compatibility check, signed subtraction `counterfactual - factual`,
  exact compatible sum, qualifying count, and missing-counterfactual coverage.
- Prediction, interpolation, factual-loss substitution, fee fallback, or missing-as-zero is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Intent precedence is explicit: locked `evaluate_label` first establishes the
  authorized trader-labelled population. Conditional `run_counterfactual`
  applies only when executing a fully specified, separately approved historical
  simulation/comparison contract. `calculate_metric` only reads or calculates
  the already approved comparison result; it does not create a counterfactual.
  After those gates, compatible intents are `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `explain_result`,
  and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing explicit label/applicability, approved method/result, compatible basis/currency, fees, or closed result.
- Treating a method-defined historical comparison as observed truth or proof
  the mistake caused the difference; invented hypothetical, factual P/L renamed
  as cost, motive/cause diagnosis, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Treat `mistake_cost` as unavailable unless the explicit label and separately
approved historical simulation/comparison contract and result all exist. The
result is a contract-defined difference, not observed truth or causal proof.
Do not default a method or rename factual P/L. Every route remains Planned.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: mistake label/version, approved counterfactual
method/version, gross versus net basis, then exact period.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which trader-authored mistake label and version should I use?"
2. If still needed: "Which approved counterfactual method version supplies the comparison result?"
3. If still needed: "Should compatible results use gross or fee-complete net P/L?"
4. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- No approved counterfactual contract/result; missing label/applicability,
  realised result, fee-complete net, compatible currency/basis, or coverage.
- Request to expose private evidence, invent an alternative outcome, assert cause, predict, or advise.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned evidence-gated labelled-item counterfactual-difference query over
  read-only review, P/L, fee, authorization, approved-method, and coverage contracts.
- No approved counterfactual/runtime metric or AI Chat handler is currently claimed.

### Result Units

- Signed money difference per qualifying labelled item and compatible aggregate
  sum in one currency, with basis, qualifying count, missing coverage, taxonomy/
  method/formula versions. Without approved comparison, result is unavailable.

### Fee Handling

- Both factual and counterfactual values use identical gross or fee-complete net basis.
- Missing/incompatible net fees make cost unavailable; no gross fallback or zero-fee assumption.

### Open-Trade Handling

- A factual open lifecycle lacks final realised P/L and cannot produce realised mistake cost.
- Decision/incomplete/missing-label/method/basis evidence remains unavailable coverage; no estimate is assigned.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show qualifying count, item differences, method/taxonomy versions, and missing-counterfactual/fee coverage.
- Any sample supports no causal, motive, discipline, predictive, or advisory conclusion.

## `setup_discipline` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit
trader-authored setup definition/version, stable decision/trade grain, and
factual applicability for every in-scope item. Each applicable item needs an
explicit trader-authored `followed` or `broken` setup determination with
provenance. The denominator is all distinct applicable items and descriptive
setup-adherence rate is followed count divided by it. Unknown determinations
remain denominator/coverage; zero denominator is unavailable. The inventory
name is retained, but no objective discipline score or inference from ticker,
pattern, duration, entry, candle, or outcome is permitted.

### Formal Wording

- "versioned trader-labelled applicable-setup followed/broken rate"
- "descriptive setup-adherence rate over all distinct applicable setup items"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I follow my labelled setup?"
- "Show followed, broken, and applicable setup trades with unknown determinations."

### Trader Slang

- "Did I stick to my setup?" needs an authored setup/version and factual labels.
- "Clean setup discipline" is not inferred from chart pattern or outcome.

### Abbreviations

- No abbreviation is globally approved.
- `SD`, `setup %`, or `A+ setup` maps only with explicit authored-definition context;
  bare `SET` or ticker-like text remains ambiguous.

### Common Misspellings

- `setup disipline`
- `set up adherance`

### Noisy or Incomplete Input

- `followed setup 80%?` needs setup/version, grain, applicability, period, and determinations.
- `SET setup wins` may be ticker/outcome text, not trader-labelled setup adherence.

### Singular and Plural Forms

- Singular: one applicable setup item with explicit followed/broken determination.
- Plural: deduplicated counts and descriptive rate over applicable setup items.

### Full Questions

- "Under my setup definition version, what fraction of applicable trades did I mark followed?"
- "Show followed, broken, denominator, unknown determinations, and coverage."

### Commands

- "Use only trader-authored setup applicability and followed/broken labels."
- "Do not recognize a setup from ticker, candles, or result."

### Sentence Fragments

- "setup discipline"
- "followed setup rate"

### Follow-Up Wording

- "Only A+ setup" narrows only when that label belongs to the authored version.
- "Include unknown determinations" preserves applicable unknowns in denominator/coverage.

### Correction Wording

- "Call it descriptive setup adherence, not an objective discipline score."
- "Use the setup version effective then, not inferred chart patterns."

### Comparison Wording

- "Compare setup adherence by month" requires the same version/grain or visible
  version splits, applicable denominators, unknown coverage, and periods.
- Setup profitability cannot stand in for followed/broken labels.

### Ranking Wording

- "Rank setups by followed rate" remains Planned and requires explicit setup
  versions, comparable applicability, denominators, coverage, and ties.
- Zero-applicability setups are unavailable, not zero percent.

### Negated Wording

- "Did not follow setup" maps only to explicit trader-labelled `broken`.
- Unknown, losing, open, or pattern mismatch does not mean broken.

### Exclusion Wording

- "Exclude inapplicable trades" follows explicit applicability.
- Applicable unknown determinations cannot be silently excluded from denominator coverage.

### Multi-Filter Wording

- "A+ setup adherence for day trades last month" requires an authored version,
  supported dimensions, stable grain, applicability, labels, and coverage.
- Outcome/pattern filters cannot create applicability or determinations.

### Multi-Part Question Wording

- "Show followed, broken, rate, denominator, unknowns, and setup versions" combines counts and coverage.
- "Explain why I lacked discipline" adds unsupported motive/diagnosis.

### Ambiguous Wording

- "Setup discipline" may mean setup adherence, profitability, frequency, or chart quality.
- "My setup" does not identify an authored definition/version or applicable grain.

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "Which setup made the most money?" is setup performance.
- "Detect A+ patterns from candles" is pattern recognition.
- "How can I be more disciplined?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, explicit/trusted period, trader-authored setup
definition/version, stable item grain, full candidate population, explicit
applicability, followed/broken/unknown determination, provenance, and coverage.

### Required Data

- Setup definition/version and stable authorized item IDs/grain with applicability.
- Trader-authored determination/provenance, review coverage, and future named query contract.

### Optional Data

- Privacy-safe setup label/evidence and effective dates.
- Separate factual setup-performance result under its own metric contract.

### Valid Filters

- Explicit period and dimensions defined by the authored setup contract.
- No outcome/pattern/state filter may infer labels or hide applicable unknowns.

### Valid Groupings

- Future grouping by non-overlapping period or explicit setup/version with compatible grain.
- Cross-account or incompatible setup-definition aggregation is invalid.

### Valid Operators

- Stable-item deduplication, applicability selection, factual-state counts,
  denominator, division, and unknown coverage; zero denominator unavailable.
- Pattern detection, outcome inference, or missing-state coercion is invalid.

### Compatible Intents

- Locked Category 1 intent `evaluate_label` is primary for the trader-authored
  setup population and followed/broken labels. `evaluate_rule` is compatible
  only when that setup separately exists as an accepted saved rule with its own
  exact rule contract; it is otherwise not routed. After label evaluation,
  compatible intents are `calculate_metric`, `summarize_performance`,
  `compare_groups`, `group_and_aggregate`, `rank_results`, `explain_result`,
  and `inspect_data_quality`.
- Every route remains Planned.

### Incompatible Combinations

- Missing setup/version/grain/applicability/determination provenance; zero denominator; hidden unknowns.
- Automated setup inference, objective discipline score, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Default Interpretation

Treat the source name as a descriptive trader-labelled setup-adherence measure
under the named version. Do not default a setup or infer followed/broken.
Missing facts return Planned/unavailable.

### Clarification Conditions

Clarify one field at a time: authored setup/version, item grain, then exact period.

### Recommended Clarification Wording

1. "Which trader-authored setup definition and version should I use?"
2. If still needed: "What decision or trade grain does that setup review use?"
3. If still needed: "What exact period should I use?"

### Unsupported Conditions

- Missing explicit setup/version, applicability, determination/provenance, coverage, or nonzero denominator.
- Request for private notes/identity, automated discipline diagnosis, motive/cause, prediction, or advice.

### Target Analytics Tool or Query Capability

- Planned trader-labelled setup-adherence query over read-only review,
  setup-definition, authorization, applicability, and coverage contracts.
- No current named runtime metric or AI Chat handler is claimed.

### Result Units

- Followed count, broken count, all-applicable-setup-item denominator, and
  dimensionless descriptive rate/percentage with unknown coverage, setup
  version, grain, period, and formula version.

### Fee Handling

- Not applicable; setup applicability/adherence cannot be inferred from P/L or fees.
- Separate setup performance preserves its own selected basis, currency, and coverage.

### Open-Trade Handling

- An applicable factual open trade counts only with explicit trader-authored setup determination.
- Open/decision/incomplete/outcome state never implies followed/broken; missing state remains coverage.

### Sample-Size Considerations

- Show followed/broken counts, applicable denominator, unknown coverage, and setup versions.
- Any sample supports no personality, discipline, causal, predictive, or advisory claim.

## `plan_adherence` Language Registry

### Exact Definition

For one server-authorized account and declared period, require an explicit
trader-authored plan version with effective scope, stable decision/action grain,
and factual applicability for every in-scope item. Each applicable item needs
an explicit trader-authored `followed` or `broken` plan determination with
provenance. The denominator is all distinct applicable items and adherence rate
is followed count divided by it. Unknown determinations remain denominator/
coverage and make the rate partial or unavailable; zero denominator is
unavailable. Performance, P/L, timing, size, ticker, notes, or outcome never
supplies the determination.

### Formal Wording

- "versioned trader-labelled applicable-plan followed/broken rate"
- "followed count divided by all distinct applicable plan decisions/actions"

### Normal Conversational Wording

- "How often did I follow my saved trading plan?"
- "Show followed, broken, and applicable plan decisions with unknown determinations."

### Trader Slang

- "Did I stick to the plan?" needs an authored plan/version and factual labels.
- "Went off-plan" is not inferred from a loss, size change, or late trade.

### Abbreviations

- `PA`, `plan %`, or `off-plan` maps only with explicit authored-plan context;

### Sentence Fragments

### Negative Examples

These examples must not map to this concept.

- "How did planned trades perform?" is a performance query.
- "How should I improve my plan?" asks for advice.

### Context Requirements

Require authorized account, explicit/trusted period, trader-authored plan
version/effective scope, stable decision/action grain, full candidate
population, explicit applicability, followed/broken/unknown determination,

# 7. Evaluation Cases Deliverable

## 7.1 Evaluation Case Schema

Evaluation Batches 1 through 7 contain exactly 462 saved and reviewed cases: 22
ordered case types for each of `C9-E1` through `C9-E21`. This is the full
production target of 22 case types for each of 21 concepts. Every object below uses the template's exact
21 keys in this order: `caseId`, `caseType`, `input`,
`expectedPrimaryIntent`, `expectedSecondaryIntents`,
`expectedCanonicalConcepts`, `expectedFilters`, `expectedGroupings`,
`expectedOperators`, `expectedComparison`, `expectedTimeRange`,
`expectedSelectedEntity`, `expectedContextRequirements`,
`expectedCapabilityStatus`, `expectedProtectedAction`,
`confirmationExpected`, `clarificationExpected`,
`expectedClarificationQuestion`, `unsupportedExpected`,
`expectedUnsupportedReason`, and `notes`.

All cases remain `Planned`. Comprehensive independent Terra review passed all
462 with zero failures. Review PASS does not mean executable or available
through AI Chat.

## 7.2 Required Case Types

Each array uses this exact order: canonical, formal paraphrase, conversational
paraphrase, trader slang, abbreviation, misspelling, noisy input, command,
fragment, follow-up, correction, comparison, ranking, negation, exclusion,
multi-filter, multi-part, ambiguity, negative example, unsupported data,
selected-entity context, and cross-category.

## 7.3 Evaluation Summary

| Case Type | Required | Completed | Passed | Notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Canonical | 21 | 21 | 21 | C9-E1-01 through C9-E21-01 passed comprehensive independent Terra review |

# 8. Coverage Report Deliverable

Section 8 reports the accepted production coverage. Comprehensive independent
Terra review passed the canonical, registry, evaluation, and coverage gates;
clerical Terra review also passed. The controller subsequently approved and
locked the category at Version 1 and marked it Complete. Neither review nor
approval creates a runtime capability.

## 8.1 Inventory Coverage

| Measure | Count |
|---|---:|
| Controlling inventory items | 21 |
| Completed items | 21 |
| Incomplete items | 0 |
| Proposed additions | 0 |
| Proposed removals or merges | 0 |
| Locked canonical names | 21 |

All 21 records remain `Planned` and their canonical names and registries are
approved and locked. Here, completed means the required planning, canonical,
registry, evaluation, and coverage material is accepted; it does not mean the
named capabilities are implemented.
